### Item 79. Quezon City dialysis centre, price not disclosed

What it says [FILED] (Nephrocare_Health_Services_Announcement_under_Regulation_30_LODR-Me_e5d1eb.txt):
- Ref NEPHROPLUS/SE/93, dated 27-Oct-2026, signed 19:35 IST by the Company Secretary (p.1).
- Nephrocare Health Care Services, Philippines Inc., the step-down wholly owned subsidiary, signed an Asset Transfer Agreement dated 27-Oct-2026 with JCM Summit Health Inc. It buys identified assets of a dialysis centre on Don Mariano Marcos Avenue, Quezon City (p.1).
- Size: "exceeds the materiality threshold" under the company's policy. The consideration "is not being disclosed, as the same constitutes confidential and commercially sensitive information" (p.1, repeated in Annexure I item 3, p.2).
- Seller not related to the promoter group; not a related-party transaction; no special rights (Annexure I items 5-7, p.2).

**The silence is the finding.** Every earlier acquisition in this series disclosed its price (Notion, event logs):
- Davao, 12-Aug: PhP 71.8M, about ₹10.4 Cr.
- Leyte, 17-Aug: PhP 75M, about ₹10.9 Cr.
- Kazakhstan, 1-Sep: KZT 561.66M, about ₹116.35 Cr.
- Bulacan, 14-Sep: PhP 67.2M, about ₹10.25 Cr.

Quezon City is the first one where the number is withheld. "Exceeds the materiality threshold" adds nothing on size. The ₹10.25 Cr Bulacan deal also crossed that threshold, so the threshold sits at or below about ₹10 Cr, and this deal is merely above it. My inference from the filed pattern. I will not estimate the price.

**What it touches on file.** The record set up a discipline test that needs these prices.
- FY26 AR read (Notion, 25-Sep, second-order link 3): "Philippines acquisition prices are rising." FY26 deals averaged about ₹6.5 Cr per clinic; the three FY27 single-centre deals cost ₹10.25-10.9 Cr each. The record said "H1 FY27 goodwill lines will tell."
- Same read, link 5: the founder's promote pays only if the PE funds hit return multiples by 31-Mar-2029, and acquisitions accelerated right after the promote was approved on 12-Aug. "Worth watching against acquisition prices."
- The 14-Sep log made combined goodwill across the four deals "the disciplined-M&A test" (Notion, 14-Sep).

This filing removes the price input from that test for the newest deal. It is dated 27-Oct, after the 30-Sep balance sheet, so the 3-Nov H1 print will not carry it either. My arithmetic on dates.

**Gate test.**
- Growth Trigger 3, Philippines consolidation toward #1: continues. Fourth Philippines tuck-in since 12-Aug. Quezon City is in Metro Manila, so this adds depth in the capital, not a new island cluster (the record already has Luzon, Mindanao and Visayas covered).
- Monitoring #12, goodwill under 15% of net worth (7.8% on file): cannot be tested on this deal until the Q3 FY27 accounts.
- Thesis-broken trigger 5, net debt added beyond ₹300 Cr: not touched. Mar-26 net cash about ₹403 Cr less the ₹147.9 Cr already disclosed in FY27 (Notion, 14-Sep table) leaves about ₹255 Cr before this deal and before H1 operating cash. My arithmetic, not filed.
- No other trigger moves.

**Two readings.**
- Bull: Metro Manila is the contested part of a roll-up. Publishing a per-clinic price hands the next seller a benchmark. Prices were disclosed four times running when the company could have withheld them, so this is a choice made for one deal, not a policy of opacity. Seller unrelated, no special rights.
- Bear: the price is withheld at exactly the point where the record found per-clinic prices rising (₹6.5 Cr to ₹10.25-10.9 Cr) and where the founder's incentive points at the next 30 months. A buyer with a disciplined price has little reason to hide it. The capital city site is also the one most likely to carry a premium.

The one observation that separates them: the Q3 FY27 results notes (about Feb-2027). The Q1 FY27 filing carried an aggregate note for seven asset transfers (₹70.93 Cr, Notion, Q1 review Cons 8). If Q3 carries the same note, and Quezon is the only Q3 Philippines deal, the price falls out. Above about ₹10.9 Cr for one centre continues the rising trend. Near the FY26 ₹6.5 Cr average says discipline. If the Q3 note aggregates Quezon with later deals so it cannot be isolated, treat that as the bear reading. The faster check is the 4-Nov call (SE/92): ask for the consideration and the treatment count.

**What it does not say.** Price; number of machines or treatments; revenue of the centre; whether real estate is included; completion conditions or date; funding source. The Bulacan and Kazakhstan filings gave at least price and, for Kazakhstan, turnover. This one gives neither.

**Internal consistency.** SE/92 on 21-Oct, SE/93 on 27-Oct: consecutive, so no exchange filing was missed between them (my arithmetic). The deal was signed inside the closed trading window (1-Oct to 5-Nov, Notion 13-Oct entry); that window binds insiders' trades, not company transactions. Not a flag.

**Price.** ₹635 (screener.in, 28-Oct-2026 06:12 IST). 52-week range ₹445-782; market cap ₹6,381 Cr, about 10.05 Cr shares, consistent with the corrected count (my arithmetic). Up 4.3% from ₹609 on file (Notion, CMP property, 22-Oct-2026). 50% above the ₹423 entry ceiling and 41% above the ₹450 early-warning alert (my arithmetic). The entry zone itself is flagged unreliable.

**Verdict.** No thesis-broken trigger fires. The deal is small in money terms and fits Growth Trigger 3. The change is in disclosure behaviour: the record's M&A discipline test now has a blind spot on the newest deal. I would add one named tripwire: a second acquisition with withheld consideration. That is a governance reading on the Entrepreneur Ledger, not a breach today. The 13-Oct ruling still stands open: run Role 1 once, after the 3-Nov print and 4-Nov call. WATCHLIST stands on your rules. That is your call.

## Paramount Comm.: deck shows domestic-led recovery, exports still below last year

Paramount is in COMPANIES MASTER at WATCHLIST / AVOID, no position ("None (AVOID at CMP)"), Promoter Verdict MONITOR. Entry Price Max and MoS are blank. The watch zone on file is about ₹15-22 (Notion, properties and Key Notes 10-Sep). Two tracker rows are linked and were read: receivables DSO plus operating CFO (Tier 1), and preferential allottees plus warrants (Tier 3).

The results were reviewed and saved on 26-Oct (Notion, "Q2/H1 FY27 results and cash gate"). Today's three items add the investor deck, the press release and the call audio link. The deck has what the results did not: order book, the export split, the company's own working-capital days, shareholding, and a Narmadapuram status page. The 37-page deck has eight image-only pages; I opened them; they are the cover and section dividers with no data (p.1, 5, 11, 13, 20, 24, 31, 33).

### 1. Exports fell YoY; the recovery is domestic [FILED, deck pp.17-18; PR p.3]

- Q2 export revenue ₹158.1 Cr, 28.2% of revenue, against ₹173 Cr in Q2 FY26, down 8.5% (deck p.17).
- H1 exports ₹313.1 Cr (29%) against ₹381.5 Cr (43%) in H1 FY26. H1 domestic ₹776.7 Cr against ₹497.0 Cr (deck p.18).
- My arithmetic, not filed: Q1 exports were about ₹155 Cr, so Q2 is flat QoQ. H1 exports are down 17.9% YoY. H1 domestic is up 56.3%.
- India B2B plus PSU was ₹349 Cr in Q2 against ₹211 Cr, up 65.1%. B2C ₹54 Cr, up 23% (deck p.17).

What it touches. Growth trigger "US recovery (tariff removed)" was ON TRACK (early) in May (Notion, concall 8A). The broker note on file says FY26 exports were EBITDA-negative and production for US orders was "paused pending October tariff decisions" (Notion, 09-Oct) [MGMT via broker]. So the margin climb from 5.2% to 7.3% came while exports shrank. The likeliest reading is mix: fewer loss-making export tons, more domestic power cable [INFERENCE]. That is good for margin now, and it means the US trigger has not started to deliver.

Note: the deck labels the whole export line "US (Exports)" (p.17), while p.16 shows four export regions. US share of exports is not given.

### 2. Order book up 19% QoQ [FILED, deck p.19; PR p.3]

- ₹731 Cr at 30-Sep, against ₹615 Cr at Q1 and ₹537 Cr at Q2 FY26. Domestic ₹632 Cr (86%). Power ₹575 Cr (79%).
- "No firm-price orders beyond 3 to 4 months" (p.19) [MGMT].
- My arithmetic, not filed: implied Q2 intake is ₹731 − ₹615 + ₹560.4 = about ₹676 Cr, book-to-bill about 1.2x. That overstates intake if B2C sales (₹54 Cr) do not pass through the book; excluding them gives about ₹622 Cr. The book covers about 3.9 months of Q2 revenue.

Against the capacity cap on file, "capped near Rs 2,200 Cr revenue" [MGMT via broker] (Notion, 09-Oct), H1 annualised is ₹2,180 Cr. A rising book against a full plant supports price and mix in H2, not volume.

### 3. The company's own working-capital days went up, not down [MGMT, deck p.27]

- "Working Capital Days 108, vs 109 days in H1 FY26" (p.27).
- On file: management said WC cycle "101 to 96 days" at Q1 (Notion tracker, Q1 concall 17-Aug) [MGMT/SECONDARY]. FY26 CCC was 101.2 (Notion, Part A Step 5).
- So on management's own measures, the cycle rose about 12 days from Q1 to H1, and is flat YoY. The definitions may differ; the deck does not define it. My 26-Oct arithmetic on net trade working capital gave about 97 days (Notion, 26-Oct).

What it touches. The Tier 1 tracker bear leg reads "receivable days sustained at/above 79 OR operating CFO negative again" (Notion tracker). The receivable leg passed at about 64-68 days. The CFO leg was met on a literal reading, ruling pending (Notion, 26-Oct). The deck's 108 days is management's own number saying the cycle did not shorten. That weakens the bull argument that the H1 cash outflow was a one-off payables catch-up. It does not fire anything new. It is evidence for the pending ruling.

### 4. Narmadapuram: management restates the date, scope differs from file [MGMT, deck pp.15, 23]

- Foundation and civil works "have commenced and are progressing well". Orders for "most critical plant and machinery already placed". Hiring underway. "Partial commissioning planned for Q1 FY28, as previously guided" (p.23).
- Scope on p.15: "EHV cables up to 220 kV", 31 acres, ₹300 Cr, FY28 ₹500 Cr, FY29 ₹1,200 Cr.
- On file: Phase 1 is EHV to 132 kV and HTLS; 220/400 kV is Phase 2 at a further ₹200-300 Cr (Notion, 09-Oct; AR Step 10). The deck puts 220 kV inside the ₹300 Cr. Either Phase 1 scope rose with no capex change, or the slide merges two phases. Not explained.
- HTLS: "₹200+ Cr HTLS order book (as on date)", capacity augmentation at Narmadapuram in FY28 (p.21).

Gate test. The 10-Sep gate was "Narmadapuram commissioning date/CWIP build" (Notion, Key Notes 10-Sep). Date leg: restated in a filed deck as Q1 FY28 [MGMT]. Build leg: still thin. CWIP ₹3.59 Cr plus capital advances of about ₹30 Cr (Notion, 26-Oct) against ₹300 Cr. "Machinery ordered" fits the capital advances. The gate stays open. The observation that settles it: CWIP plus capital advances at 31-Dec-2026 (Q3 balance sheet is not mandatory; the H2 balance sheet at 31-Mar-2027 is). By my threshold, below about ₹150 Cr spent at 31-Mar-2027 makes partial commissioning in Q1 FY28 hard to believe.

### 5. Institutional holding closes the UA question [FILED, deck p.36]

- Sep-2026 shareholding: promoter 45.87%, FPI 1.89%, DII 4.13%, public 48.11%. Shares 32,72,39,473.
- FII plus DII is 6.02%. The UA 1.25x multiplier needs below 3% (Notion, Part B 1B). It does not apply. The tracker note predicted this (Notion tracker, allottees row).
- Promoter 45.87% matches the post-equity-only figure on file (Notion, 25-Jun dilution table). So the 72 lakh promoter warrants are still unconverted.

Consequence: the 12x top of the 9-12x range was described as "full ROCE recovery to 15% + UA" (Notion, Part B 1B). The UA half is now off. Any move to 12x must come from the ROCE pillar (about 13.7% annualised operating ROCE, Notion 26-Oct) or the cash pillar. That is your call.

### 6. What the deck does not say

- No FY27 revenue or margin number. The roadmap says only "move toward pre-tariff margins" (p.32). H1 revenue growth is 24.1% (deck p.26), ahead of the 15-20% May guide and the ~15% broker guide on file.
- No reason for the other-expense reversal, which was the 26-Oct open item.
- No US share of exports, no tariff status, no data-centre revenue (p.22 has approvals only).
- No capex to date on Narmadapuram.
- The call was held 27-Oct (Tuesday), and the audio link was filed the same day at 18:35 (An_2b296c, p.1). No transcript yet; due about 3-Nov [INFERENCE on the rule].

### Inconsistencies

- 4-year revenue CAGR is 35.4% on p.6 and 34.7% on p.9.
- The "64.6% 4-year PAT CAGR" (p.6) still ends on FY26 PAT that includes the ₹27.83 Cr keyman payout. The May review flagged this (Notion, concall Step 1). P.9 says FY26 "one-time items weighed on margins". The main one-time item raised profit.
- Q2 FY26 revenue is ₹427.6 Cr in the PR and deck (standalone) and ₹428.00 Cr in the consolidated results on file. The PR's +31.1% and the file's +30.9% are both right on their own basis (Valens was still consolidated in Q2 FY26). Not a flag.

### Price

₹65.6 (screener.in, 28-Oct-2026 06:12 IST). 52-week ₹28.3-75.7, market cap ₹2,145 Cr. My arithmetic, not filed:

- Up 10.1% from ₹59.6 on file (screener.in, 26-Oct 20:30 IST), after the deck and the call.
- 24.9x H1 annualised diluted EPS (₹2.63), 22.6x Q2 run-rate (₹2.90) (EPS from Notion, 26-Oct).
- 1.9-2.7x the re-run value of about ₹24-35 on file. 3.0x the top of the ₹15-22 zone.

### Verdict

No formal thesis-broken condition exists (Role 2 never run; Notion, Part B). The deck confirms the operating recovery and adds a growing order book. It also shows the recovery is domestic, with exports still below last year, and management's own working-capital days at 108, not shortening. The UA route to a higher multiple is closed. The price rose 10% on this batch and moved further from any value on file. AVOID unchanged, with the earnings re-run and the CFO ruling still pending. That is your call.

## Borana Weaves: the 192 looms moved from Dec-26 to Mar-27

Borana is in COMPANIES MASTER as WATCHLIST, but the page calls that a placeholder. It is a Phase 2 record, unsigned, with no entry, no MoS and no valuation run (Notion, status banner). No tracker rows are linked. The page says the Role 5.5 rows are "not written, pending sign-off".

What the filing says (Borana_Weaves_Board_Meeting_Intimation_for_Approval_Of_e98ce3.pdf, p.1) [FILED]:
- Board meets Monday 2-Nov-2026 to approve Q2 and H1 results for the period to 30-Sep-2026.
- Same meeting will "consider and approve the installation of additional 192 Water Jet (WJ) Looms" with ancillary machinery at Unit 4, "targeting commercial production by March, 2027".
- No capex amount. No funding source. No total loom count after the addition.

What it touches on file. Notion logs about 1,212 looms today and "next 192 looms due Dec-2026" (Notion, Dominant variable 3). The FY28 inflection case rests on "full-year 192 looms + full-year hybrid" (Notion, FY27E section). This filing has the board only now being asked to approve 192 looms, with production three months later than the logged date.

Two readings. The filing does not settle which one is right.
- Same 192 looms, late. Dec-2026 becomes Mar-2027, and the board is only approving them now. If so, the "Unit 4 delivered ahead of schedule twice" credit on the advantages table (Notion, Competitive advantages) takes its first miss. FY27 loses whatever Q4 loom output the base case counted. My arithmetic, not filed: 192 / 1,212 = 15.8% more looms. A quarter of that on a ₹410 Cr base is at most about ₹16 Cr of FY27 revenue, and less after ramp-up. The FY28 test still holds if production really starts in March, because FY28 starts 1-Apr-2027.
- A second tranche of 192 on top of the first. That would mean 1,212 + 384 = 1,596 looms, not 1,404 (my arithmetic). This is bigger capacity than any filing on file shows. The page calls the forum talk of Units 5/6/7 "not corroborated by any filing". This one is still Unit 4, so it does not settle that question either.

The observation that separates them is the 2-Nov board outcome. Watch for three things: the post-expansion loom count (1,404 or 1,596), the capex figure, and whether the first 192 are described as installed. If the outcome gives no count, ask for it at the Q2 call.

The same meeting delivers the falsifier. The page names Q2 FY27 gross margin as "the single observation that resolves the whole estimate" (Notion, FY27E):
- Near 41% (FY26 was 41.37%): the margin is real cost position, not import timing. The model is wrong and must be rebuilt.
- Near 38-39%: the import-window read is confirmed and FY27 is the trough year (base PAT ₹57-60 Cr against FY26's ₹64.61 Cr).
- Primary falsifier: EBITDA margin above 23% through Q2 and Q3. Q2 is the first of the two legs.

The page also says Q1 FY27 volumes and utilisation were withheld (Notion, Live-verification). Check whether the Q2 pack restores them. A second quarter without them makes it a pattern, not a lapse.

What the intimation does not say. It does not mention a trading window closure. The board also takes a capex decision on the same day as results, before any cash flow for the half is public. That is routine for Indian SMEs-turned-mainboard, but the capex should be judged against H1 operating cash flow. The page logs CFO/PAT at 68.7% for FY26.

Price: ₹217 (screener.in, 28-Oct-2026 06:10 IST), 52-week range ₹206-420, market cap ₹579 Cr. My arithmetic, not filed:
- 33% below the ₹324.5 logged on 09-Sep-2026.
- About 5% above the 52-week low.
- About 10x the base-case FY27 EPS of ₹21.7 and 12x the bear-case ₹18.0.
- No entry line exists to test it against.

Nothing on file explains a one-third fall in seven weeks. The SEBI matter at R&B Denims is the obvious candidate. I have not checked it, and I will not guess.

Verdict: no falsifier fires yet. The loom timing is a likely first slip on the one promoter skill the page credits, unless the 2-Nov outcome shows it is a second tranche. Sign-off, valuation and entry are all still open. That is your call.

## The others, briefly

### Transrail Light: Q2 gate print set for 3-Nov, book leg already fails

Transrail is in COMPANIES MASTER at WATCHLIST / BUY ON DIPS. Entry ₹415, MoS ₹336, trim line ₹520 (Notion, properties and 28-Jul entry). The record shows "HOLD 3% hard cap" (02-Sep, 06-Aug entries), but Avg Cost is blank and the 23-Oct addendum says position status and cost are not confirmed. I read both linked tracker rows: the Q3 intake separator and the Phase 2 capacity slip.

What it says [FILED] (Transrail_Light_Board_Meeting_Intimation_for_Considerati_d28645.txt, p.1):
- The board meets Tuesday 3-Nov-2026 to approve standalone and consolidated unaudited results for the quarter and half year ended 30-Sep-2026.
- The trading window has been closed since 1-Oct and stays closed till Thursday 5-Nov-2026, both days inclusive.
- Signed by the CS, Monica Gandhi, at 17:58 on 27-Oct, after market hours. Seven days' notice, inside the Reg 29 minimum. No timing lapse this time, against three lapses logged on the page (30-Sep "172,400", NOPL outcome lag, 20-Oct/21-Oct release).
- One feed error, not in the filing: the Screener summary says "half-year FY26". The filing says the half year ended 30-Sep-2026, which is H1 FY27.

The notice itself is routine. The date matters. The page has been waiting on this print as the re-underwrite gate since 06-Aug. Two places on file expect it later. The NOPL separator (20-Oct entry) says "about mid-Nov-2026". The Phase 2 capacity tracker row has its Next Check Date at 15-Nov-2026. The answer now arrives 3-Nov, 34 days after quarter end (my arithmetic).

This is also the first print that can test the cash leg. Q1 had no cash-flow statement, so cash conversion was logged INDETERMINATE (Notion, 06-Aug entry). A half-year result has to carry the balance sheet and the cash-flow statement [INFERENCE from the Reg 33 half-year format; the notice does not say so].

The Q2 re-underwrite gate, leg by leg, before the print (Notion, Key Notes 22-Sep and 01-Oct; my arithmetic, not filed):
- **Book stops contracting: fails in practice already.** Book at end-Q1 was ₹15,635 Cr and Q2 intake about ₹575 Cr. For the book not to shrink, Q2 revenue would have to be at or below ₹575 Cr, about a third of Q1's ₹1,702 Cr. That will not happen on any reasonable print.
- **Intake above ₹1,500 Cr: depends on the reading.** Per quarter, Q2 at about ₹575 Cr fails. H1 cumulative, ₹1,609 Cr passes. The ruling has been open since 01-Oct. It decides only how Q2 is scored; Q3 to date (₹1,747 Cr) passes either way.
- **H1 CFO/PAT at least 0.8x: open until 3-Nov.** Q1 reported PAT was ₹107.88 Cr (Notion, 06-Aug). If Q2 PAT is near Q1, H1 PAT is about ₹216 Cr and H1 CFO needs about ₹173 Cr or more. That assumes Q2 PAT equals Q1; it is not a forecast. Read it with the trade acceptances line (about ₹1,200 Cr, treatment undisclosed; 06-Aug entry). A CFO figure flattered by more vendor financing should not count as a pass. Net debt was ₹466 Cr at Q1 against the ₹274 Cr anchor.

So the gate cannot pass as written even before the numbers come out. Strike two from 01-Oct stands.

What else the 3-Nov print decides:
- **The probability re-weight.** Q1 was strike one, below bear on two metrics, and the rule needs two consecutive quarters (06-Aug entry). Q2 revenue growth against the 20-22% guide is the test. Q2 FY26 revenue is not on the page, so I cannot set the exact line.
- **Thesis-broken trigger 1** (EBITDA margin below 10.5% for two quarters). Q1 was 11.9%. FY27 margin guide is about 11% (02-Jul entry). A Q2 below 10.5% would start that count, not fire it.
- **NOPL separator** (20-Oct entry). Total equity commitment to NOPL above ₹300 Cr with no EPC margin disclosed confirms the bear reading. An NOPL EPC order at the 11.5-12.5% band with equity at or below ₹200 Cr supports the bull reading.
- **Phase 2 capacity row.** A revised date for 196,000 MTPA tower and 49,500 KM conductor, or none.

What the notice does not say: no earnings call date. That matters for the Phase 2 and NOPL questions. Also still missing since 29-Sep: the QIP and MOA postal ballot result. With the window closed until 5-Nov, a QIP launch before the print looks unlikely [INFERENCE].

Price: ₹431 (screener.in, 28-Oct-2026 06:14 IST). The 52-week range is ₹401-741, and market cap is ₹5,800 Cr. That is down 8.1% from the ₹469 on file (22-Oct), 3.9% above the ₹415 entry, 28.3% above the ₹336 MoS, 17.1% below the ₹520 trim line and 7.5% above the 52-week low (my arithmetic). No filing in this window explains the fall.
- One stale line is now crossed. The Position Size property still reads "add below ₹450; MoS ₹430". At ₹431 the price is below that ₹450, but the line was superseded on 28-Jul by "add only below ₹415". The governing line is not crossed. Also, the re-underwrite rule says do not add on price alone before the Q2 gate (22-Sep entry). Please let me tidy the property so it cannot be misread.

Verdict: zero of 4 thesis-broken triggers fire. Nothing changes until 3-Nov. The price is close to the ₹415 add line just as the gate print lands, so the order matters: the gate is read first, then the price. That is your call.

### Tata Power Co.: ₹250 Cr BPCL MoU, immaterial; de-leverage still open

Tata Power is in COMPANIES MASTER as HELD at ₹232 average cost. Entry ₹300, MoS ₹240, position "Medium (4-6%), currently 5% notional x 4.5x MTF leverage = 22.5% gross. De-leverage protocol active" (Notion, properties). One tracker row is linked (TPREL quarterly commissioning), and I read it. Two items.

Item 143, BPCL MoU [FILED, voluntary] (92adbe.txt):

- The cover letter says the release "is not a disclosure in terms of the requirements of Regulation 30" (p.1). That is the same as the 27-Aug, 08-Oct, 12-Oct and 14-Oct filings (Notion).
- Tata Power will provide 110 kV connectivity and build the power infrastructure for a 90 MW load at BPCL's PRFCCU unit, Mumbai Refinery (p.2).
- "Combined estimated outlay of approx. ₹250 crore for Tata Power and BPCL", about 24 months, "subject to applicable approvals" (p.2) [MGMT]. The split between the two is not given.
- Also planned: a 400 kV transmission facility at Trombay "over the next five years" (p.3). No capex, no approval status.

Tested against file (my arithmetic, not filed):

- Monitor #11 (capex within the ₹15 to 25K Cr a year band): ₹250 Cr combined, over two years, is at most 1.7% of one year at the low end. Tata Power's own share is smaller and not disclosed. It does not touch the band.
- Monitor #12 (EM evidence moving from verbal to documentary): an MoU with no split and "subject to approvals" is the verbal end. It does not move.
- ₹250 Cr is 1.8% of BPCL's ₹14,203 Cr project outlay (p.2). The load is real, but small for Tata Power.
- Mumbai transmission is regulated, so this capex should earn a regulated return once approved [INFERENCE]. That fits the "defensive regulated T&D" line in Section 9. It is too small to show.

Boilerplate (p.3) is still word for word the 31-Aug release: 26 GW+, 17.7 GW clean, 8.8 GW thermal, 7,800+ CKM, 13 Mn customers, 4.9 GW cell and module, 2.8 GW PSP, 7,000+ EV points in 717 cities. That is eight weeks without change, after the 12-Oct TPREL release moved operational capacity to 7.2 GW (Notion, 13-Oct log). Drafting lag, not a flag [INFERENCE]. The 4.3 GW against 4.9 GW ambiguity from 08-Oct is unresolved.

Item 142, investor meetings (ab7cce.txt p.1) [FILED]: 1:1 and group meetings in Chennai on 30-Oct-2026, no UPSI to be shared. The trading window has been closed since 24-Sep and results are on 11-Nov (Notion, 08-Oct log). Meeting investors 12 days before results is routine. It touches no gate. One thing to watch: whether new independent directors are named before 11-Nov, the open check from 14-Oct. This filing does not say.

Price: ₹339 (screener.in, 28-Oct-2026 06:13 IST), 52-week ₹333 to ₹465, market cap ₹1,08,354 Cr. Notion on file ₹345 (screener.in, 14-Oct-2026). My arithmetic:

- Down 1.7% from the page, 1.8% above the 52-week low.
- ₹39 (13%) above the ₹300 add line, inside the ₹300 to ₹380 WATCHLIST band for fresh buyers (Notion, Section 7).
- 46% above the ₹232 average cost, 21.5% below the ₹432 de-leverage sale price.
- Below the FY28 bear FV of ₹471 (Notion, Section 5).

None of the six thesis-broken triggers fires. HELD unchanged. The question carried since 3-Jul is still the one that matters, and neither filing touches it: was the de-leverage protocol (sell 75 to 80% at ₹432, repay MTF) executed? At ₹339 it is still not confirmed. That is your call.

### Arisinfra Solu.: two small orders; JS Infra's is the one to read

Briefly, but tested. Arisinfra is in COMPANIES MASTER as HELD, "Medium 4% (entered ₹106 on 09-Jul-2026...)" (Notion, Position Size). Entry Price Max ₹110, MoS ₹88, CMP on file ₹131 (screener, 24-Oct). The 29-Sep exit or cut-to-Small recommendation is still unruled, 29 days on (Notion, 24-Oct Rulings). Three items, all filed 27-Oct.

#### Items 7 and 8: third Transcon DaaS mandate, GDV ₹250 Cr

What was filed [FILED]: AUSPL ("Unitern") got a service order from Transcon Iconica Pvt Ltd, a Transcon SPV, for DaaS services on Transcon Ramdev Plaza, Santacruz (W). Estimated GDV ₹250 Cr. Initial term 18 months, extendable. Domestic, not a related party (Arisinfra_..._167e9e.pdf, p.2-3). It is Unitern's third Transcon mandate after Phase I (completed, OC received) and Transcon UNO at Kalina. Total DaaS GDV "over ₹2,750 Cr" (86908e, p.2).

Tested against the file:
- It confirms DaaS sits in AUSPL, as the 24-Oct entry settled (Notion, 24-Oct "Corrections").
- The "broad consideration" field in the Reg 30 annexure gives only the project GDV (p.2). GDV is the developer's sales value, not Aris's fee. The order value to Aris is not disclosed. Silence on the one number Reg 30 asks for.
- GDV portfolio: "crosses ₹2,000 Cr" on 7-Sep (Notion, 08-Sep entry) to "over ₹2,750 Cr" now. This mandate is ₹250 Cr of the ₹750 Cr added in seven weeks. Transcon UNO is not in the Notion record. I cannot see whether UNO was filed under Reg 30. Check.
- Material pull-through: Vaishnavi came with "₹100+ Cr" of supply (Notion, 08-Sep). Here construction is far along and no pull-through number is given. It is likely small [INFERENCE].
- The CEO's quote says CATALYST creates "liquidity for both the developer and its lender" (p.2). That suggests a project that needed rescue on sales and cash [INFERENCE]. AUSPL's FY26 receivables were about 239 days (Notion, 24-Oct entry). A collections-and-lender mandate sits close to the developer's cash. The filing does not say whether Unitern puts money in ("Money, Material and Management", p.3).
- Concentration: three mandates from one group. Transcon is on file as a strategic partner (Notion, Gate 0 Block F).
- Small oddity: the press release went out at 17:30 and the Reg 30 order letter at 18:19. The annexure gives no date of award, so the 24-hour timing cannot be checked.

Effect on the thesis: supports growth trigger #2 (DaaS scaling) on GDV. It does not touch debtor days, the over-6-month bucket or margin. GDV is not revenue (Notion, 08-Sep Read).

#### Item 9: JS Infra Core, ₹9.85 Cr work order

What was filed [FILED] (611034, p.2): JS Infra Core (70% subsidiary) got a work order from S.P. Adke's Construction for "Infra construction work at Bhagur under GE Deolali", about ₹9.85 Cr, to be completed in 17 months. Domestic, not a related party.

Why it matters more than its size:
- The postal ballot asking you to approve a ₹500 Cr loan and guarantee cap for JS Infra is open now (e-voting 26-Oct to 24-Nov). Its justification is that "business growth has surpassed previous projections" (Notion, 24-Oct, ballot p.15, p.24). This order was filed the day after voting opened.
- My arithmetic, not filed: ₹9.85 Cr over 17 months is about ₹7 Cr a year. That is about 2% of the ₹500 Cr cap, and about 0.9% of FY26 consolidated revenue of ₹1,067 Cr. Q1 FY27 parent lending into JS Infra was already ₹30.62 Cr (Notion, 24-Oct), about 3x this whole contract. JS Infra's net worth is ₹0.105 Cr.
- Scope: the ballot describes JS Infra as supplying asphalt and road materials (Notion, 24-Oct). This is construction work, taken as a subcontract from another contractor. "GE Deolali" reads as a Garrison Engineer (Military Engineer Services) works contract [INFERENCE]. So JS Infra's payment depends on the main contractor's collections from the government, one tier removed.
- The counterparty's form and credit are not disclosed.

The order does not support a ₹500 Cr cap on its own. It does show the subsidiary is moving from materials supply into civil execution, which needs working capital, as the cap request was being voted. Neither point fires anything. Both bear on your ballot vote.

#### Gates and price

- Thesis-broken triggers: none touched (debtor days over 160 twice, over-6-month bucket over ₹100 Cr, margin under 7.5%, discounted WC raise).
- Pre-committed trim rule (Q1 consolidated debtor days over 150 or the over-6-month bucket over ₹90 Cr means trim to 2%): still untestable. Q1 consolidated debtor days are not on the page, now 12 weeks after the 5-Aug print.
- The separator set on 24-Oct is unchanged: the H1 consolidated balance sheet (Q2 results due by 14-Nov). Debtor days over 150, or standalone loans to subsidiaries over about ₹250 Cr (from ₹195 Cr), confirms the bear case.
- Price: ₹141 (screener.in, 28-Oct-2026 06:10 IST). 52-week range ₹82.2-175, market cap ₹1,153 Cr. My arithmetic: up 7.6% from ₹131 on file; 33% above your ₹106 cost; 28% above the ₹110 entry; above the ">₹130" avoid line (Notion, Recommendation). About 21.9x FY26 parent PAT of ₹52.70 Cr. At ₹141 the AUSPL swap shares are worth about ₹95.7 Cr, or about 12.3x AUSPL FY26 PAT. That is still well below ASL's own multiple.

Verdict: no trigger fires. The DaaS mandate is real but unsized. The JS Infra order is small next to the cap you are being asked to approve. On your rules neither settles the 29-Sep exit or cut-to-Small question. The JS Infra filing is one more input to the ballot vote due by 24-Nov. That is your call.

Item 92. Vikas Basra has been designated Senior Management Personnel, Chief AI Officer, effective 26-Oct-2026, as a full-time employee [FILED] (R_Systems_Intl_Announcement_under_Regulation_30_LODR-Ch_954375.txt, p.1-2). What it touches on file:
- Trigger 3 (AI-led services). The share is 29% of revenue, against a target of 35-40% by CY27-28. Confirms if above 35% by end CY26, fails if it stalls at 30% (Notion, Section 3B). A senior AI hire is an input to this trigger, not evidence for it.
- Risk 3. Frontier-model and PE-consortium entrants commoditise mid-market AI delivery. Management's answer, given twice, is that "AI is a people problem", with 1,400+ certified staff as the moat (Notion, Concall Verification, Anthropic/PE section). A hire from a direct product-engineering peer fits that framing [INFERENCE].

It does not touch the master gate. That gate failed on 05-Aug: no organic constant-currency number, a third Novigo evasion, ACV forward dodged, and margin guided down to about 18% (Notion, Q2 concall overlay, Section 6). The standing instruction is "hold starter, do not add" until organic growth and Novigo are disclosed. This filing changes neither.

What the filing does not say:
- Whether the role is new or replaces someone. The page records no previous AI head.
- Who he reports to.
- Whether any RSU or ESOP grant comes with the role. Grants under the Management Incentive Plan 2023 are a dilution line on file (Notion, Dilution Schedule, Instrument 3).
- How the role sits next to the Velotio technology leadership. Velotio's co-founder Chirag Jog was its CTO (Notion, Dilution Schedule). OCRPS vesting depends on the Velotio founders staying. If a new AI chief above them led to a founder exit, vesting would fall, and so would dilution [INFERENCE, no evidence either way].

The annexure numbers its rows a), b), a), b). That is cosmetic.

Two readings, both thin:
- Bull: a peer's CTO-level AI leader joins to run OptimaAI and EXIQO productisation. HFS has already validated that stack (Notion, 29-Jul press release review). This supports the fixed-price capture mechanism in Trigger 2.
- Bear: a senior cost added in the quarter management guided sustainable margin down. The title is cheap to give. AI-share claims remain management-reported with no audited split.
- What separates them: the AI-led revenue share reported at end CY26. Above 35% confirms the trigger. At or below 30% means it has stalled (Notion, Section 3B).

- 42.2% below the 52-week high. Up 6.3% on the stale ₹237 on file (08-Jul-2026).

### Atlanta Electric: Q2 board on 31-Oct sets the 17% margin test

Atlanta Electricals is in COMPANIES MASTER as WATCHLIST / BUY ON DIPS, with no position. Entry Price Max ₹1,105, MoS ₹891, AVOID above ₹2,000 (Notion, properties; AR deep dive 01-Sep). No tracker rows are linked.

Item 13 is a one-page Reg 29 intimation, read in full [FILED] (Atlanta_Electric_Board_Meeting_Intimation_for_To_Consider_4ad519.pdf, p.1):
- The board meets Saturday 31-Oct-2026 to approve unaudited standalone and consolidated results for the quarter and half year ended 30-Sep-2026.
- The trading window, closed per the 25-Sep-2026 letter, stays closed until 48 hours after the results.
- Signed by Tejal S. Panchal, CS & Compliance Officer, 27-Oct-2026 14:29 IST.

Checks on the filing itself:
- Notice period: 28, 29 and 30 Oct are three working days between intimation and meeting, above the two-day Reg 29 minimum [INFERENCE on the count]. Compliant with room.
- The Screener summary says "half-year FY2026". The filing says the half year ended 30-Sep-2026, which is H1 FY27. Aggregator error, not the company's.
- A Saturday board means the market reacts on Monday 2-Nov.

This filing dates the gate the page has been waiting for. The page calls Q2 FY27 the "next master decision gate" (Notion, Key Notes 01-Sep). Here is what the 31-Oct print must be tested against, leg by leg:

1. Standalone operating EBITDA margin, ex other income, against 17%. Q1 was 16.63% SA and 16.53% CON, the first sub-17% quarter (Notion, Q1 FY27 review, Step 1C).
- At or above 17%: scale-to-Medium condition (iii) is met. With (i) and (ii) already met, the original three-leg gate passes.
- Below 17%: second consecutive sub-17% quarter. That trips item 3 amber (2 of 2) and the "Conditions for Re-Evaluation" line "two consecutive quarters of EBITDA margin below 17%" (Notion, Section 8 and Re-Evaluation).
- Depends on reading between 15.5% and 17%: the CMD's 15.5 to 16% "stable margin" anchor would be met while the CFO's 17 to 18% would be missed (Notion, guidance ledger G2 vs G2′, ruling C1 never answered). A print of 16.0 to 16.9% passes management's one guide and fails the other. Please rule now on which guide is operative, before the number arrives.
- The exit trigger (below 15% for two consecutive quarters) cannot fire on Q2 alone, because Q1 was above 15%.

2. H1 cash flow and balance sheet. This is the "named missing evidence" that holds cash conversion at INDETERMINATE (Notion, Q1 review Step 5). Net WC days were 74.8 at FY26 against a green band of 60 to 75 (Notion, AR deep dive, Section-8 status). The CEO warned WC days would move to the 80s and 90s.
- Two readings: a reading above 75 is amber on the checklist, but it was pre-announced, so it is a credibility pass if it lands inside 80 to 90. Above 90 breaks the CEO's own range.
- Thesis-broken trigger 5 (CFO/PAT below 0.70x cumulative for two years) is annual and cannot be tested on a half.

3. Revenue against the FY27 base. Base FY27 revenue is ₹2,740 Cr (Notion, 22-Jul forward update). Q1 was ₹466.33 Cr, 17.0% of base. My arithmetic, not filed: FY26 Q2 plus Q3 together were ₹788.78 Cr (₹1,851.32 Cr less Q1 ₹315.11 Cr less Q4 ₹747.43 Cr). The page has no separate Q2 FY26 figure, so the YoY base for this quarter is NOT FOUND on file. G1, 40% growth, implies about ₹2,591 Cr for FY27.

4. Order book against the DA early-warning line "OB below ₹2,400 Cr by Q2 FY27" (Notion, DA Section 8). Last on file: ₹3,116.63 Cr at Q1.

5. Dated milestones the print or call should touch: Atlanta Trafo first revenue (about Q2 FY27, Notion monitorables); the 765 kV tie-up (gate (iv) needs a signing by end-Q3 FY27; proposed trigger (f) fires if unsigned by end-Q4 FY27); IDT +5,000 MVA by about 10-Dec-2026 (G5); AE Components capex start (thesis Q2 FY27).

What the filing does not say: no call date. Q1 had a call; the guidance-conflict ruling depends on one.

Price: ₹1,634 (screener.in, 28-Oct-2026 06:10 IST). 52-week ₹708 to ₹2,200, market cap ₹12,567 Cr. My arithmetic, not filed:
- ₹12,567 Cr ÷ ₹1,634 = 7.69 Cr shares, which ties to the 7.69 Cr on file.
- 47.9% above the ₹1,105 Entry Price Max and 83% above the ₹891 MoS. 18.3% below the ₹2,000 AVOID line.
- Down 5.4% from the ₹1,728 on file, which is the 14-May analysis price. That Notion price is five months stale.
- To the ₹1,932 base FY29 fair value (Notion, AR deep dive), ₹1,634 implies about 5.7% a year over three years, against the 25% mandate.

So even a clean Q2 does not make a buy at this price. It moves the size gate, not the entry. No thesis-broken trigger fires. Decision Status stays WATCHLIST / BUY ON DIPS. That is your call.

What it says [FILED] (MTAR_Technologie_Disclosures_under_Reg_291_of_SEBI_SAST_R_03138a.pdf):
- 17 Motilal Oswal MF schemes held 15,12,718 shares (4.9179%) before. They bought 50,264 shares (0.1634%) on the market on 21-Oct-2026. That takes them to 15,62,979 shares (5.0813%) (p.2 to p.4).

- Not promoter group (p.2).

Tested (my arithmetic, not filed):
- 15,12,718 + 50,264 = 15,62,982, not the 15,62,979 filed. That is a 3-share gap. The percentages recompute on 15,62,979, so the "before" or "acquired" line is off by 3 shares. Immaterial, but the form does not add up.
- At ₹6,742 the stake is worth about ₹1,054 Cr. The 21-Oct buy was about ₹34 Cr at today's price. The trade price is not given.

Part of this holding is index and momentum money. It follows price and index weight, not the thesis [INFERENCE].

What it does not say:
- The per-scheme split. Every row says "Please refer Annexure" (p.2, p.3), and no annexure is in the 4-page PDF. So passive vs active weight cannot be measured.

The Sep-26 shareholding pattern, due by 21-Oct, is still not on file. It answers both the 29.23%-vs-29.35% question and the distance to 25%.
- No gate, trigger or tracker falsifier is touched. A 5% holder does not test Bloom concentration or cash conversion.

### Northern Arc: results on 3-Nov, earlier than the tracker date

Northern Arc is in COMPANIES MASTER at WATCHLIST / BUY ON DIPS, not held. Entry ₹173 (range ₹153 to ₹173), MoS ₹138 (Notion, properties and Entry zone). I read both linked tracker rows: the AUM pace row and the SMBC row.

What it says [FILED] (Northern_Arc_Board_Meeting_Intimation_for_Considerati_dff20c.pdf, p.1):
- The board meets Tuesday 3-Nov-2026 to approve standalone and consolidated unaudited results for the quarter and half year ended 30-Sep-2026.
- The trading window stays closed till 5-Nov-2026, 48 hours after the results.
- The notice is filed under Reg 29(1) and Reg 50(1), and is copied to Catalyst Trusteeship. So it also goes to holders of listed debt.

The notice itself is routine. The date matters. Since 06-Oct, Notion has named Q2 results as the observation that separates the two readings. The page said "due by mid-Nov", and the AUM tracker row has its Next Check Date at 14-Nov (Tracker, AUM pace row). The answer now arrives 11 days earlier. Results come 34 days after quarter end (my arithmetic).

The tests, pre-registered from the page (my arithmetic, not filed, unless marked):
- **AUM, checklist #4 and thesis-broken "FY27 AUM growth below 15%".** Q1 AUM was ₹16,855 Cr. To be on pace for +15% by Mar-27 needs about ₹743 Cr a quarter, so about ₹17,598 Cr at 30-Sep. The tracker's falsifier says "below 2% QoQ again (AUM below about Rs 17,400 Cr)". Those two numbers do not match. 2% QoQ is about ₹17,192 Cr; ₹17,400 Cr is +3.2%. Please rule which line the falsifier means.
- **A marker already on file.** At Bharat Connect on 5-Oct, management put total AUM at "~Rs 17,000 Cr" [MGMT via broker] (Notion, Bharat Connect 09-Oct). If that is the Sep-26 figure, Q2 growth is about +0.9%. That is below all three lines above. The "~" is rounded, so treat this as [INFERENCE] until 3-Nov. The same session guided FY27 AUM growth of 22-23%. From ₹16,594 Cr at March, 22.5% needs about ₹20,328 Cr by Mar-27. That is about ₹1,158 Cr a quarter from June, nearly 4.5x the Q1 pace. The guide and the ~₹17,000 Cr figure cannot both hold without a very strong H2.
- **Credit cost, checklist #1 and the SHARED CATALYST trigger.** Green at or below 2.8% for two quarters, red above 3.2%. Management guided 2.6-2.8% [MGMT via broker]. Q1 credit cost was never disclosed. Q2 should give the first quarterly figure. The trigger needs credit cost above 3.2% AND GNPA above 1.5%. Q1 GNPA was 1.00%.
- **Exit RoE, thesis-broken "below 11% for two quarters".** On Jun-26 TNW of ₹3,906 Cr, 11% is about ₹107 Cr of PAT a quarter. Q1 was ₹114 Cr. Q2 PAT below about ₹107 Cr would be quarter one of the two. A rising TNW raises that bar slightly.
- **PCR, checklist #3.** Not disclosed in either CARE release. It is a live trigger: "PCR below 50% while GNPA rises".
- **Cost of funds, checklist #9.** The blended figure is still NOT FOUND. ₹25 Cr of CP matures on 9-Nov (Notion, 23-Oct entry). That is the first rollover after results, and its coupon is the next marker for the amber-leaning-red leg.

What it does not say: no earnings call date, and no "other business". Neither is required in a Reg 29 notice. The window closure start date is not given.

SMBC row: not touched by this filing. The 25-Oct auto note says the falsifier fired on an aggregator read (SMBC 3.76%, not 7.84%) [SECONDARY]. It still needs the NSE shareholding filing to confirm it.

Price: ₹247 (screener.in, 28-Oct-2026 06:12 IST). 52-week ₹206 to ₹334, market cap ₹3,998 Cr. This is unchanged from ₹247 on file (Notion, 24-Oct). It is 42.8% above the ₹173 entry and 79% above the ₹138 MoS. On Jun-26 TNW the stock trades at about 1.02x book against your 0.97x fair P/B. Fair P/B on the same book is about ₹234. My arithmetic. Price sits just above fair value and far from entry.

No thesis-broken trigger fires on a meeting notice. The name stays where it is. The 3-Nov print decides the AUM leg. The rulings already open (RoA measure, AUM test basis, Decision Status against the body verdict, CP coupon as CoF evidence) are best settled before 3-Nov. That is your call.

- Bear: allotment is still "subject to receipt of requisite regulatory approvals" (results note 10, Notion 26-Oct). Nothing has been paid. The ₹325 Cr upfront is about the size of the H1 rise in net debt (₹47 Cr to ₹361 Cr, Notion 26-Oct). A promoter whose shares are 100% pledged now has a reason to slow an above-market purchase.
- The observation that separates them: the allotment filing and receipt of the ₹325 Cr upfront at ₹231.55, unchanged. ICDR allotment runs within 15 days of the last approval [INFERENCE on ICDR]. If allotment is filed by the Q3 results (about Feb-2027) on unchanged terms, the bull reading holds. Any delay past that, a change in terms, or an upfront funded from the 360 One facility (the tripwire's third limb) confirms the bear.

Verdict: the filing is hygiene. The price is not. The market's first read of the Q2 print is a 9.5% fall, and the promoter warrant has moved from a cheap option to an above-market commitment. No thesis-broken trigger fires because no model is signed. The pending rulings (falsification line, monitorable 2, monitorable 7) are where the decision sits. That is your call.

  - The ₹278.8 Cr payables build. Short-term borrowings rose from ₹234 Cr to ₹462 Cr. Warranty provisions were ₹12.5 Cr and slow-moving stock ₹8.0 Cr, against ₹2.2 Cr and ₹1.2 Cr a year earlier (Notion, 26-Oct review).
  - DENSO close date and P&L geography. eDrive was still wholly owned at 30-Sep.
- The archetype rule on file depends on this call. A C1 miss "while continuing to withhold ROCE/CFO" slides the archetype toward EVASIVE (Notion, Role 5 merge). ROCE and CFO are now disclosed. So the call's tone on C1 is the open half of that test.

#### Item 130: ESOP allotment of 10.07 lakh shares, arithmetic ties

What it says [FILED] (Sona_BLW_Precis_Announcement_under_Regulation_30_LODR-Al_1e54c5.pdf, p.1):
- On Tuesday 27-Oct-2026 the NRC allotted 10,07,121 equity shares of ₹10 under the Sona ESOP Plan 2023 to employees who exercised vested options.
- Shares go from 62,41,70,274 to 62,51,77,395. Paid-up capital goes from ₹6,24,17,02,740 to ₹6,25,17,73,950.
- Signed 21:28 IST on 27-Oct.

The test (my arithmetic, not filed):
- 62,41,70,274 + 10,07,121 = 62,51,77,395. Ties. Capital rises ₹1,00,71,210, which is 10,07,121 × ₹10. Ties.
- Dilution is 0.16%. That is immaterial to the base FY29 EPS of ₹16.
- The opening count, 62,41,70,274, is exactly the closing count after the 4-Oct allotment (Notion, 5-Oct review). So nothing was allotted between 4-Oct and 27-Oct. The record is continuous for once. The 26-Oct save deferred a share-base note of paid-up ₹6,241.28 mn at 30-Sep. That is the pre-4-Oct count, so it also fits.
- Size and timing. This batch is 24 times the 4-Oct batch of 41,850 shares. It was allotted the day after results, inside the closed trading window that runs to 28-Oct (Notion, 13-Oct review). Exercising and allotting during a closed window is allowed; selling is not until the window opens. Not a flag. The likely read is that employees exercised in size once the stock was near its 52-week high [INFERENCE].
- At ₹852 the batch is worth about ₹85.8 Cr (my arithmetic).

What it does not say:
- The exercise price, the number of employees, and the cash received. H1 ESOP cash was ₹107.6 Cr (Notion, 26-Oct review, p.15). That works out to roughly ₹470 a share across about 23 lakh shares, but the rounded capital figures make this inexact [INFERENCE]. The ESOP charge in the P&L depends on the grant fair value, which is not given here.
- This is not the MD's Performance Share Plan 2025 grant of 1,70,747 shares at face value (Note 4b, logged 26-Oct). That grant still has no allotment filing in the record.

Gates. No gate is touched. No thesis-broken trigger fires: ROCE below 14% for two years, or a forced block sale or control dispute.

#### Price: the market paid up for a quarter the gate did not clear

₹852 (screener.in, 28-Oct-2026 06:13 IST). 52-week range ₹438 to ₹868. Market cap ₹53,170 Cr. My arithmetic, not filed:
- That is up 9.0% from ₹782 on file. The ₹782 was the 26-Oct close, before the after-market results (Notion, 26-Oct review). So this is the first price that reflects the results. It is 1.8% below the 52-week high.
- It is about 2.2x the ₹384 base FV, 4.3x the ₹197 entry, 4.6x the ₹185 buyable line, and about 71x the ₹12.0 TTM EPS on file.
- The bull ceiling on file is EPS ₹20.6 × 22.5x = about ₹464 (Notion, 26-Oct review). The price is 84% above it.
- The screener market cap uses the pre-allotment share count. On 62,51,77,395 shares it is about ₹53,265 Cr. Immaterial.

Two readings of the price move:
- Bull: the market is buying what the gate does not measure. Revenue was +35%, BEV was 51% of automotive revenue, H1 FCF was ₹445 Cr, and ROCE was up to 16.4%.
- Bear: the gate legs that set the destination multiple did not move. Margin was flat near 23% for two quarters and missed bull. Cash passed on payables. The EV share of the order book eased for a third print. Each 9% move up widens the gap to a fair value the quarter did not lift.
- The one observation that separates them is the same as on file: the Q3 FY27 operating margin on the rev-ops basis (around late January 2027). At or above 24.0% with payables flat or down, the market is early, not wrong. Below 23.0% would mean the market paid for a re-rating the numbers did not deliver.

Verdict. Two hygiene filings, one of which is the answer source for an open ruling. No thesis-broken trigger fires. On your rules the name stays at AVOID, and the price has moved further from every line on file. That is your call.

### Blue Jet Health: CARE withdraws rating, bank debt fully repaid

Blue Jet is WATCHLIST, not held (Position Size "None"). Entry Price Max ₹230, MoS ₹184, Promoter Verdict TRUSTWORTHY (Notion, properties). The 23-Sep Key Note already asked for the ₹230 entry to be re-derived or retired. No tracker rows linked.

#### Item 16. CARE rating withdrawn, no dues outstanding

What it says [FILED] (Blue_Jet_Health_Rating_update_14e4d0.txt):
- CARE withdrew its ratings on Blue Jet's long-term / short-term bank facilities with immediate effect, because the facilities were "repaid... in full" and a no-dues certificate was received from the lenders (p.1). Amount rated now: nil (p.2).
- The withdrawn rating was CARE A+; Stable / CARE A1+, assigned 16-Dec-2025 (p.2). So the rating lived about ten months.
- CARE's financial table (basis not stated) (p.1):

| ₹ Cr | FY25 (A) | FY26 (A) | Q1 FY27 (UA) |
|---|---|---|---|
| Total operating income | 1,048.29 | 983.43 | 293.11 |
| PBILDT | 396.03 | 330.20 | 98.08 |
| PAT | 305.20 | 247.82 | 78.26 |
| Overall gearing (x) | 0.02 | 0.03 | NA |
| Interest coverage (x) | 4,041.15 | 52.92 | 148.61 |

**Arithmetic against the record** (Notion, Section 2 base case; my arithmetic, not filed):
- FY26 actual against the FY26E base: revenue ₹983 Cr vs ₹940 Cr (+4.6%); EBITDA ₹330 Cr vs ₹290 Cr (+13.9%); PAT ₹248 Cr vs ₹225 Cr (+10.1%). The trough year came in above base on every line.
- Q1 FY27 annualised: revenue about ₹1,172 Cr, EBITDA about ₹392 Cr, PAT about ₹313 Cr. Against the FY27E base of ₹1,030 / ₹330 / ₹247 Cr, that is 14%, 19% and 27% above, and revenue is above even the bull FY27E of ₹1,100 Cr. One quarter, annualised from the best quarter of a rising run (₹165, 192, 235, 293 Cr, Notion 23-Sep), so treat it as direction, not a forecast.
- Q1 PBILDT margin 33.5%, FY26 33.6%. Matches "OPM back to 33%" on file.

**The interest line is the odd part.** Interest implied by CARE's coverage ratio (my arithmetic, assuming coverage = PBILDT / interest): FY25 about ₹0.10 Cr, FY26 about ₹6.2 Cr, Q1 FY27 about ₹0.66 Cr. So a company carrying about ₹410 Cr net cash (Notion, EM G1, Q3 FY26) borrowed enough in FY26 to pay roughly ₹6 Cr of interest, got rated in Dec-25, and has now repaid everything. The filing does not say what the borrowing was for. Working capital during the trough is the obvious candidate: the record logs working capital days at 124-157 and a Block B cash-generation deal-breaker [INFERENCE]. If so, full repayment is consistent with working capital being released as sales recovered, which is the leg the Gate 0 cap rests on. Not proven by this filing.

**Gate test.** Thesis-broken conditions (Notion, Section 4):
- 1, PI segment below ₹150 Cr: not testable; no segment data.
- 2 and 3, Mahad validation and Vizag Phase 1 timing: not touched.
- 4, ROCE below 15% for two quarters: not testable.
- 5, CFO/PAT below 0.30x for two years: not testable here; the debt repayment is a weak positive hint only.
- 6, pledge or OFS above 11%: not touched.

None fires. The EM line "G1 War Chest: A+/A1+ rated" loses its rating label, not its substance: zero bank debt.

**Fair value.** The ₹450 3-year and ₹628 5-year base fair values rest on FY27E PAT ₹247 Cr and EPS on 17.35 Cr diluted shares (Notion, Sections 2 and 4). The Q1 run-rate argues those earnings are too low. The share count argues the per-share figures are too high: market cap ₹9,380 Cr at ₹496 implies about 18.91 Cr shares, and the 23-Sep AGM note logs 189,275,701 filed shares. That is about 9% more than the valuation uses. On 18.93 Cr shares, FY26 EPS is ₹13.09, not ₹14.28. My arithmetic. The two errors cut in opposite directions, the same pattern the Nephrocare record found on 25-Sep. Net direction unknown without a Role 1 re-run.

**Odd item, not a flag.** The CARE PDF link stamps "202610131032" while the press release is dated 26-Oct-2026 (urls.json). Probably an upload path [INFERENCE].

**Price.** ₹496 (screener.in, 28-Oct-2026 06:10 IST). 52-week ₹325-688; market cap ₹9,380 Cr. Down 16.2% from ₹592 on file (Notion, CMP property, 22-Sep-2026). Still 2.16x the ₹230 entry max and 10% above the ₹450 3-year base fair value, so inside the record's own "AVOID zone above ₹450" (Notion, verdict card). Trailing PE on FY26 PAT about 38x; on the Q1 run-rate about 30x; destination PE 22.85x (my arithmetic).

No trigger fires. The filing is a credit non-event and a mild cash positive. The useful by-product is the earnings run-rate and the share count, both of which say the entry line on file is stale, as the 23-Sep note already said. WATCHLIST stands on your rules. That is your call.

### Sansera Enginee.: QIP clean on paper, ₹218 Cr GCP idle two years

Sansera is in COMPANIES MASTER only as a tracking note (25-Sep AGM, 03-Oct promoter sale and ESOP). There is no thesis, no gate and no Decision Status, so there is no thesis test (Notion, page body). No tracker rows linked.

What it says [FILED] (Sansera_Enginee_Announcement_under_Regulation_30_LODR-Mo_038e22.pdf), ICRA monitoring report for Q2 FY27 on the ₹1,200 Cr QIP of October 2024:
- "No deviation" from the objects (p.1, p.2). Net proceeds ₹1,174.979 Cr (p.3).
- Used by 30-Sep-2026: ₹974.306 Cr, of which ₹30.000 Cr was used in the quarter. Unused: ₹225.694 Cr (p.6).
- By object (p.6): issue expenses ₹25.021 Cr, done; debt repayment ₹700 Cr, done; capex at existing plants ₹100 Cr, done; land for a new plant ₹92.591 Cr of ₹100 Cr (₹7.409 Cr left); general corporate purposes (GCP) ₹56.694 Cr of ₹274.979 Cr (₹218.285 Cr left).
- Idle money sits in four HDFC and Axis fixed deposits plus the monitoring account: ₹261.603 Cr, including ₹35.909 Cr of interest earned (p.7).

Tested (my arithmetic, not filed):
- The parts tie. ₹225.694 Cr unused + ₹35.909 Cr interest = ₹261.603 Cr parked. The FDs sum to ₹261.040 Cr, plus ₹0.563 Cr in the account. The GCP line items (p.9) sum to ₹56.694 Cr. The capex tranches (p.8) sum to ₹100.0 Cr.
- GCP is 20.6% used, two years after the issue. The offer document scheduled it for FY25 (p.8). Land was also due in FY25 and is still ₹7.4 Cr short.
- Interest of ₹35.9 Cr on idle QIP money is about 3% of net proceeds.

What is odd inside it:
- The delay table (p.8) marks land and GCP as "Delay", then gives "N.A." for the length of the delay. Both the reason and the proposed course of action are blank. The board comments column says "No comments" throughout. A two-year delay with no reason is a disclosure gap, not a deviation.
- Every answer in the arrangements table (p.4) is "As confirmed by the Issuer's management". ICRA says it does no audit and no independent check (p.2). "No deviation" means the uses match the headings, not that the uses were tested.
- The GCP uses (p.9) name no counterparty: renewable energy ₹11.68 Cr, an associate ₹15 Cr, and a subsidiary ₹30.012 Cr. The subsidiary line is against a ₹50 Cr board approval from 27-May-2025, of which ₹30 Cr went in this quarter. The subsidiary is not named. It may be the ADS (aerospace, defence, semiconductor) arm that got its own board-level CEO on 24-Sep (Notion, tracking note) [INFERENCE]. The filing does not say.
- Small error: p.8 says "in Q3 FY2026, company has unutilised INR 10.000 crore", where p.9 shows ₹10 Cr was used. "Unutilised" is a typo for "utilised".
- Timing is clean. ICRA signed 26-Oct, the company filed 27-Oct, inside the 45-day window after the quarter.

Cross-check with the 03-Oct note: market cap ₹26,781 Cr at ₹4,291 gives about 6.24 Cr shares (my arithmetic). So the 10,000-share promoter sale logged on 03-Oct was about 0.016% of the company. That open item ("cannot size the stake") can now be sized. At ₹4,291 the ESOP strikes sit 50.1% (₹2,142.40) and 11.8% (₹3,782.60) below market.

Price: ₹4,291 (screener.in, 28-Oct-2026 06:13 IST), 52-week ₹1,471 to ₹4,744. That is 5.4% below the ₹4,537.50 logged for 1-Oct (Trendlyne LTP, Notion 03-Oct note). No lines on file to test against.

Who it matters to: anyone screening Sansera on the ADS hypothesis. The ₹30 Cr into an unnamed subsidiary, and ₹20 Cr more approved, is the money trail to ask about. No change. Decision Status is blank and operator-only.

- **What it does not say.** No named speakers. The record logs a CEO speaking on the Q1 call (Notion, sections 7 and 9). - **Same signatory again.** MC Garg, Director, signs this, as he signed the 27-Sep and 22-Oct filings. Not a flag.

## Not in COMPANIES MASTER

### Larsen & Toubro: record orders, flat EBITDA, profit lifted by other income

Not in COMPANIES MASTER. Six filings; items 62 and 63 are byte-for-byte the same board outcome file (33 pages, same text), filed twice. Item 60 is only a link to the call audio. Item 64 is 21,098 ESOP shares (face value ₹2, so about ₹0.4 lakh of capital, no dilution that matters; 1,65,811 shares were allotted in the whole quarter, Outcome p.11 note i). So the substance is the results (62/63), the presentation (61) and the Dhaka Metro order (65).

**The headline, in numbers [FILED]**
- Revenue ₹78,750 Cr, up 16% (Q2 FY26 ₹67,984 Cr). H1 ₹1,46,692 Cr, up 11% (Outcome p.9, p.11).
- Consolidated PAT to owners ₹5,202 Cr, up 33% (₹3,926 Cr). H1 ₹9,325 Cr, up 24%. EPS ₹37.81 for the quarter, ₹67.78 for H1 (Outcome p.11).
- Order inflow ₹1,47,665 Cr, up 28%, a quarterly record. H1 ₹2,55,678 Cr, up 22%. Order book ₹8,53,514 Cr (presentation p.5, p.33), up 28% on Sep-25 (₹6,67,047 Cr) and 15% on Mar-26.
- Net working capital 1.7% of revenue against 10.2% a year ago. ROE 16.7% against 17.2% (presentation p.5).
- Auditor MSKA: limited review, unmodified conclusion (Outcome p.21-23). No exceptional item this quarter. FY26 carried a ₹1,722 Cr consolidated exceptional loss (Outcome p.11).

**Where the 33% PAT growth came from (my arithmetic, not filed)**
Profit before tax rose from ₹6,336 Cr to ₹7,641 Cr, a gain of ₹1,305 Cr. Split:
- EBITDA: +₹39 Cr (₹6,806 Cr to ₹6,845 Cr, up 1%). EBITDA margin fell from 10.0% to 8.7%.
- Other income: +₹922 Cr (₹1,384 Cr to ₹2,306 Cr).
- Finance cost: down ₹290 Cr. Depreciation: down ₹55 Cr.
So other income is about 71% of the PBT gain, and the operating business added almost nothing. The company's own words (presentation p.9-10): finance cost and depreciation fell "due to non-consolidation of Hyderabad Metro"; other income was helped by "higher group surplus funds and improved yields and one-time gain on divestment of SWC business in LTTS". The size of the SWC gain is NOT disclosed.

Below PBT, the tax rate helped too. Tax was ₹1,726 Cr on ₹7,641 Cr, an effective 22.6%, against 26.0% a year ago. A deferred tax credit of ₹337 Cr sits inside it (Outcome p.11). Non-controlling interest was flat at ₹747 Cr.

So the "PAT up 33%" line is real money but it is not an operating-margin story. Take out the other income gain and the lower tax rate and PAT growth falls well below the revenue growth of 16%. That cannot be sized precisely because the SWC gain is unstated.

**Margins by segment, Q2 FY27 against Q2 FY26 [FILED, presentation p.15-19]**
- Infrastructure & Utilities: 5.7% against 6.2%. Reasons given: cost pressure in water projects and more jobs below the margin-recognition threshold.
- Energy Conventional: 7.4% against 8.2%. Reason: new CarbonLite jobs in early stages (CarbonLite margin 5.8% against 19.0% a year ago, which included variation claims).
- Energy Green: 6.0% against 6.2%.
- Manufacturing & Products: 15.7% against 13.5%. The one clear gainer, on Precision Engineering & Systems.
- Projects, Products & Manufacturing as a whole: 7.0% against 7.3%.
- Technology, Platforms & Services: 19.5% against 20.0%. Financial Services EBITDA 26.2% against 25.0%.

**The order inflow record rests on a few very large orders**
Energy Conventional took ₹78,413 Cr, more than double last year's ₹38,349 Cr (Outcome p.5). Of that, Hydrocarbon was ₹65,770 Cr (₹657.7 bn) and CarbonLite ₹11,440 Cr (presentation p.33). The presentation says "Ultra Mega and Mega orders", which L&T defines as above ₹15,000 Cr and ₹5,000 to ₹10,000 Cr (order classification table, press release p.3). That one segment is 53% of the quarter's inflow (presentation p.13).

Everything else shrank (my arithmetic): total inflow less Energy Conventional is ₹69,252 Cr against ₹77,435 Cr a year ago, down about 11%. By segment:
- Infrastructure & Utilities ₹35,509 Cr, down 13%, "deferment of select targeted orders".
- Energy Green ₹6,558 Cr, down 45%, "deferrals in the Solar business".
- Realty down 12%, "launch deferrals".
Only Manufacturing & Products (+34%) and Technology (+12%) grew outside the big hydrocarbon wins.

Geography matters more than usual. International orders were 63% of the quarter's inflow (₹92,375 Cr, Outcome p.4). From the presentation charts the Middle East was about 51% of Q2 inflow (p.13) and 40% of the order book (p.14) [chart reading from the text layer; please eyeball p.13-14]. 40% of ₹8,53,514 Cr is about ₹3.4 lakh Cr (my arithmetic). Yet the Outlook paragraph on Outcome p.8 says "The Middle East continue to pose varied downside risks to global growth and investment activity". The book is more concentrated in the region the company itself flags. That is the main concentration point in this set.

**Execution lags the book**
Book-to-bill for the quarter is 1.88x (₹1,47,665 / ₹78,750, my arithmetic). Trailing revenue of ₹3,01,117 Cr (presentation p.23) against the ₹8,53,514 Cr book is 2.8 years of cover. Revenue growth of 16% is well below book growth of 28%, so most of the new order wins are not yet in revenue. Q1 revenue was ₹67,942 Cr (Outcome p.11), so Q2 was up about 16% quarter on quarter (my arithmetic) and Q1 had grown only about 7% year on year (₹67,942 Cr against ₹63,678 Cr, my arithmetic). Q2 is a step up, not a steady run-rate yet.

**Cash flow quality: the "record low" working capital needs a second look**
Presentation p.29-30, excluding Financial Services:
- Q2 cash from operations ₹12,840 Cr against ₹4,760 Cr (up 170%). H1 ₹17,990 Cr.
- Of the Q2 figure, ₹8,290 Cr is a working capital release. It was only ₹420 Cr in Q1. So about 65% of Q2 operating cash flow is working capital (my arithmetic), and 48% for H1.

The balance sheet (Outcome p.12) shows how: other current liabilities rose ₹15,079 Cr in six months (₹85,862 Cr to ₹1,00,940 Cr), more than the ₹9,551 Cr rise in other current assets. Trade receivables fell ₹2,742 Cr. L&T does not label the liability line, but on the usual EPC pattern it holds customer advances and contract liabilities. That is my inference, not filed. Advances from ultra-mega orders arrive up front and are consumed as the work is done.
- Bull reading: genuine discipline, plus large advances on a long book, so cash stays strong while the jobs run.
- Bear reading: NWC at 1.7% is flattered by advances that unwind as execution rises, so cash conversion normalises downward in H2/FY28.
- What separates them: group NWC to revenue at March 2027. If it stays below roughly 5% (my threshold, not L&T's), the bull reading holds. If it climbs above that while order inflow slows, it is the bear case.

Balance sheet otherwise: group cash and investments ₹97,400 Cr (₹974 bn) against ₹80,400 Cr in March (presentation p.26-27). Gross debt/equity 1.02 against 0.95; net debt/equity 0.33 against 0.35. The gross rise is the financing arm: borrowings in Financial Services ₹1,23,300 Cr against ₹1,09,800 Cr.

**Portfolio moves buried in the notes [FILED, Outcome p.11, p.16 notes]**
- Nabha Power divested 25-Jun-2026.
- Hyderabad Metro: sale agreement of 29-Apr-2026 with the Telangana government's HMRL. Completion date "revised to December 31, 2026", so it has already slipped once; the earlier date is not in these pages. It stays "held for sale" (assets held for sale ₹16,341 Cr, liabilities ₹15,715 Cr). The sale consideration is not in the pages I read.
- Data Center & Cloud business and the L&T Network Services stake agreed on 11-Aug-2026 to be sold to Vyoma.AI Limited, a wholly owned subsidiary, completion expected in Q3 FY27. A sale to a wholly owned subsidiary is a reorganisation, not a monetisation.
- Realty undertaking: scheme to move it to L&T Realty Properties (wholly owned) is awaiting NCLT approval, appointed date 1-Apr-2026.
- A ₹500 Cr tokenised NCD (Demat 2.0 format) was placed privately on 9-Sep-2026, fully used, no deviation (Outcome p.32). No dividend was declared with these results.

**What it does not say**
- No operating margin guidance and no revenue or order guidance for FY27. The Outlook is macro only; it notes the RBI raised the repo rate 25 bps to 5.50% and moved to "calibrated tightening".
- The size of the SWC gain and the Hyderabad Metro sale price.
- Names, clients and values for the ultra-mega orders. The Q2 inflow detail is by business line only.
- Presentation p.5 and p.6 print ROE 16.7% "includes 100 bps impact of Labour Code provision recognised in Q3 FY26", so the year-ago 17.2% and today's 16.7% are not like for like. Minor.
- The earnings call itself (27-Oct) is audio only. Item 60 gives a link, no transcript. Management commentary on H2 margins, the Middle East exposure and the advances unwind is where this will be settled. Transcript not yet in the file.

**Dhaka Metro Line 1 (item 65) [FILED press release, Ann. p.2-3]**
Two design-and-build packages from Dhaka Mass Transit Company (JICA-funded): CP09 track works and electrical systems (with Japan's Sumitomo as partner) and CP10 signalling, telecom and platform screen doors. Order class "Major", meaning ₹5,000 to ₹10,000 Cr in total. Order value itself is NOT stated, the class does not say whether the range is per package, and L&T's share against Sumitomo's on CP09 is not stated. Against the ₹8,53,514 Cr book, even the top of the range is about 1.2% (my arithmetic). It is dated 27-Oct, so it counts in Q3 inflow, not Q2. This is a repeat customer (L&T did Dhaka Line 6) and non-Indian again, which adds to the international weighting above.

**Price context**
₹3,665 (screener.in, 28-Oct-2026 06:11 IST), market cap ₹5,04,279 Cr, 52-week range ₹3,288 to ₹4,440. It sits about 17% below the high and 11% above the low. On trailing consolidated EPS of ₹129.86 (FY26 ₹116.93 less H1 FY26 ₹54.85 plus H1 FY27 ₹67.78, my arithmetic) that is about 28x. This price is the pre-results close.

**Who it matters to**
Anyone holding Indian capex and EPC names. The sector read: a record order year driven by Gulf hydrocarbon, a domestic infrastructure inflow that fell, and flat operating profit. No thesis test here as L&T is not on file.

**Cross-links to names on file**
- Transrail Lighting (on file, WATCHLIST per the 1-Oct review; board meets 3-Nov-2026 for Q2, item 145). L&T's Power T&D business is named among the sources of high-value Q2 orders (Outcome p.4), and L&T's Infrastructure & Utilities segment orders fell 13% on deferrals. Transrail's own intake gate (the Q2 intake test) will be judged against that background. L&T's H2 commentary on transmission tenders is the quickest read-across to Transrail's bull case that tenders bunch into H2.
- Asian Energy (on file) and Deep Industries (in this run): the Gulf offshore and onshore hydrocarbon boom behind L&T's ₹65,770 Cr of hydrocarbon inflow is the same capex cycle that oilfield-services names rely on, though they are domestic. A loose read-across only.
- Tata Power (in this run, items under that name): L&T's Energy-Green solar deferrals and 45% inflow drop are a counterpoint to solar demand claims.

### Wework India: the ₹65.8 Cr profit is ₹20.5 Cr on statutory accounts

Wework India is not in COMPANIES MASTER (notion_page null), so there is no thesis, gate or tracker row to test. This is an explanation of the filings, with the checks I would run on any new name. Six items were read in full: the 40-page investor presentation (idx 165), the press release (166), the 31-page shareholders' letter (167), the board outcome (168), the results (169) and the call-recording notice (170). Items 168 and 169 are the same 12-page document. The four image pages in the presentation and the two in the letter are section dividers and a cover photo. They carry no data.

#### The headline basis is not the statutory basis
Every headline number is "IGAAP-equivalent": cash rent charged as paid, no Ind AS 116 lease accounting. The press release says so only in a note at the end (Press release p.4). The reconciliation sits in the annexure (Letter p.17-18; Presentation p.30-31). [FILED]

| Q2 FY27, ₹ Cr | Headline (IGAAP-equivalent) | Statutory (Ind AS) |
|---|---|---|
| EBITDA | 162.9 | 508.7 |
| PAT | 65.8 | 20.5 |
| H1 PAT | 119.0 | 16.3 |
| Q1 PAT | 53.2 | (4.2) loss |

- Gap in Q2 is ₹45.3 Cr. In H1 it is ₹102.7 Cr. The "PAT +149.5% YoY" headline compares ₹119.0 Cr with ₹47.7 Cr. On statutory numbers H1 FY26 was a ₹7.8 Cr loss, so the true change is a swing from loss to a ₹16 Cr profit.
- The Ind AS statement itself (Results p.9, consolidated) shows Q2 PAT of ₹20.4 Cr, Q1 a loss of ₹4.1 Cr, H1 ₹16.4 Cr. The slides round these slightly differently (₹20.5 Cr, (₹4.2) Cr, ₹16.3 Cr). That is rounding, not a restatement.
- What the adjustment is (my arithmetic, not filed): the IGAAP expense line adds ₹328.1 Cr of rent and brokerage. Cash rent paid is ₹298.2 Cr and brokerage accrual is ₹29.1 Cr (Letter p.19). The sum is ₹327.3 Cr, so the IGAAP charge is cash rent plus accrued brokerage. Ind AS charges ROU depreciation ₹230.2 Cr plus lease interest ₹152.7 Cr = ₹382.9 Cr (Presentation p.31). The ₹45.3 Cr gap is that difference net of small items.
- Which is more true? Lease rents usually escalate. Cash rent in early years is below the straight-line average, and new centres often have rent-free fit-out periods. So the IGAAP basis flatters profit while the portfolio is young and growing fast. This is [INFERENCE]. The company's own line is that it avoids "non-cash rent straight-lining". The adjustment fell from ₹57.4 Cr in Q1 to ₹45.3 Cr in Q2 even as desks rose 8.8%. That is a mild sign the gap is narrowing, but one quarter is not a trend.
- Tax: IGAAP PAT is effectively pre-tax PAT. H1 tax is ₹0.1 Cr (Presentation p.32). The balance sheet carries a ₹307.7 Cr deferred tax asset (Presentation p.37). While that shelters profit, headline growth gets a free lift. At a normal rate PAT would be about a quarter lower. [INFERENCE]

#### Quality of the statutory profit
- "Other income" on the Ind AS statement is ₹11.1 Cr in Q2 and ₹18.5 Cr in H1 (Results p.3, standalone). The H1 cash-flow statement lists lease-termination gain ₹5.2 Cr, gain on sale of assets ₹2.8 Cr, fair-value gains ₹5.3 Cr, mutual-fund gains ₹4.7 Cr and others. These sum to about ₹18.4 Cr, matching the line almost exactly (Results p.5).
- Standalone H1 Ind AS PBT is ₹14.9 Cr. So without these gains it would be about ₹3.5 Cr negative (my arithmetic, not filed). Q2 on the same basis: ₹19.4 Cr less ₹11.1 Cr = about ₹8.3 Cr.
- IGAAP strips most of this out (Finance and other income cut from ₹35.5 Cr to ₹12.9 Cr in H1), so the headline is cleaner on this one point. The statutory figure is the one more exposed to one-off gains.

#### What is genuinely good
- Revenue ₹768.7 Cr (+31.6% YoY, +10.1% QoQ). Core operations ₹657.7 Cr (+31.7% YoY). Digital ₹29.2 Cr (+45.6%) (Presentation p.22). [FILED]
- Centre-level EBITDA ₹214.0 Cr, 28.4% margin, up 109 bps YoY (Presentation p.24).
- Cash rent actually paid is up 21.4% YoY (₹298.2 Cr vs ₹245.6 Cr, Letter p.19) against revenue up 31.6%. That is real operating leverage on a cash basis.
- Mature centres (over 12 months) at 87.7% occupancy, up 355 bps YoY. Breakeven occupancy is stated at 55.7%.
- Members 121.7k, +32.5% YoY against capacity +26.9%. Net debt moved from ₹310.5 Cr a year ago to net cash of ₹23.1 Cr. Credit rating A+, cost of borrowing 8.5%.
- Statutory results carry an unmodified limited review from S.R. Batliboi (Results p.2, p.8). Subsidiaries and the associate (MyHQ Anarock) are tiny: associate profit share is ₹0.9 Cr in Q2.

#### Tests on the headline claims
1. Free cash flow. FCFO ₹237.7 Cr is "1.5x EBITDA" (237.7 / 162.9 = 1.46x). It is built from Ind AS operating cash flow ₹590.4 Cr, less cash rent ₹298.2 Cr, less actual brokerage ₹54.5 Cr (Presentation p.28). Note brokerage paid (₹54.5 Cr) was almost double the accrual (₹29.1 Cr) in Q2.
   - H1 standalone operating cash flow includes a working-capital inflow of about ₹125.9 Cr (sum of the adjustments, Results p.5). The biggest items are other liabilities +₹70.8 Cr and other financial liabilities +₹75.8 Cr. The filing does not say what these are (member deposits, advances and landlord incentives are my guesses).
   - If that inflow is excluded, H1 FCFO would be roughly ₹254 Cr, not ₹379.8 Cr (my arithmetic, not filed, standalone against consolidated, so approximate). The FCFF turn to positive (₹16.0 Cr for H1) would then not survive.
2. Capex. H1 capex is ₹363.8 Cr (Q1 ₹188.0 Cr, Q2 ₹175.8 Cr). Q2 capex fell even though Q2 was the record desk-opening quarter (11.7k desks against about 6.7k in Q1, the H1 18.4k less Q2 11.7k). So capex per opened desk is not a stable figure: Q1 about ₹2.8 lakh, Q2 about ₹1.5 lakh, H1 about ₹2.0 lakh (my arithmetic). Spend leads openings. At about ₹2 lakh per desk, the roughly 47k desks still to come would need around ₹900 Cr over the coming years (my arithmetic). Next quarter's FCFF depends on how much of that falls in H2. A new ₹34.25 Cr, 10 MWp captive solar plant also gets final board approval (Board outcome p.1), about 9% of H1 capex. The filing gives no saving, no ownership structure and no start date.
3. "Net cash". The ₹413.3 Cr "cash and equivalents" (Presentation p.29) is mostly mutual funds. Reported cash on the balance sheet is only ₹6.0 Cr; current investments are ₹378.7 Cr (Presentation p.37). Those two plus bank balances ₹5.8 Cr add to about ₹390 Cr, so about ₹23 Cr of the company's "cash" figure is not identifiable in the statements (my arithmetic; it may sit in deposits under other financial assets). Net cash of ₹23.1 Cr excludes lease liabilities of ₹5,233 Cr (₹4,520 Cr non-current plus ₹713 Cr current) against total equity of ₹342 Cr (Presentation p.37). On Ind AS the balance sheet is leveraged about 15x. Net cash is a correct statement on the company's definition and a thin one.
4. Order book. Locked-in core revenue is ₹3,843 Cr, up 66.1% YoY. Locked-in rent is ₹1,421 Cr, up 60.6% YoY (Presentation p.20). The slide says contract value is "growing faster than rent". That is true over the year. Quarter on quarter it is not: revenue +14.3% against rent +23.4%, and the ratio slipped from 2.9x (June) to 2.7x (September). The slide also shows "revenue/rent multiples 2.7x | 3.0x" (Presentation p.13). The 3.0x is not defined. I could not reproduce it: Q2 revenue over cash rent is 768.7 / 298.2 = 2.58x, and over IGAAP rent plus brokerage it is about 2.34x (my arithmetic).
5. Lease cover. Remaining locked-in rent is ₹1,421 Cr for operating centres. The Ind AS lease liability is ₹5,233 Cr. The ₹3,800 Cr gap is the part beyond the lock-in period or on centres not yet open. The filing does not split it. It tells you how much of the liability rests on the company expecting to stay past the lock-in. [INFERENCE]
6. Value-added services lumpiness. VAS was ₹79.6 Cr in Q2, up 38.4% QoQ and 35.2% YoY. Q1 was only ₹57.5 Cr, below Q2 FY26's ₹58.9 Cr. VAS (events, F&B, customisation, tech) supplied ₹22.1 Cr of the ₹79.2 Cr QoQ revenue increase, 28% (my arithmetic). Customisation can be one-off.
7. Employee cost fell QoQ (consolidated ₹58.3 Cr to ₹55.8 Cr, Results p.9) while desks rose 8.8% and revenue 10.1%. This helped Q2 margin. The filing gives no reason.

#### Occupancy: the record quarter is not a record in occupancy
- Portfolio occupancy is 83.7%, down 114 bps QoQ from 84.9%. The letter's own history shows the March 2026 record of 86.9% (Letter p.15). So occupancy is about 320 bps below the March peak. The company says the dip is "new capacity still filling" (Letter p.5).
- Growth centres (12 months or younger) hold 25.0k desks and 16.2k members, 64.8% occupied (Presentation p.21). That leaves about 8.8k empty desks there, against about 23.6k empty in the whole portfolio, so 37% of all vacancy (my arithmetic). Their occupancy was 64.9% in June, so it has not improved even though the cohort grew from 15.6k to 25.0k desks. Breakeven is 55.7%.
- Another 4.5k desks opened in the first week of October (Presentation p.12), so Q3 starts at about 149.8k desks. Signed leases and LOIs cover about 47k more desks, 32% of today's capacity, targeting 190-195k desks (Presentation p.18).

#### Two readings
- Bull: The expensive capex cycle is mostly paid, with ₹363.8 Cr funded from operations. Each new cohort fills from about 50% to 87% over about a year. Mature occupancy is rising, the order book is up 66%, and cash rent is growing slower than revenue.
- Bear: Headline profit is on a basis that flatters young portfolios. Statutory PAT is ₹20 Cr on ₹768 Cr of revenue (2.6% margin). H1 cash flow got about a third of its FCFO from working-capital inflows. Occupancy has slipped for two quarters, with 47k more desks to fill. About 47% of core revenue is from North American-domiciled members and 66.3% from global members (Presentation p.18), which is a sentiment exposure to US-linked capability-centre hiring (inference).
- The single observation that separates them is Q3 FY27 (December quarter). Look for growth-centre occupancy above about 70% and portfolio occupancy back above 85%, with the IGAAP-to-Ind AS PAT gap below about ₹40 Cr. That is my own threshold, not a company one.
