If growth-centre occupancy is still around 65% with another 4.5k to 10k desks added, the bear reading gains.

#### Price context
₹626 (screener.in, 28-Oct-2026 06:14 IST). 52-week range ₹420 to ₹794, so 21% below the high. Market cap ₹8,695 Cr. The IPO offer-for-sale price works out to about ₹648 (₹29,996.43 million over 46,296,296 shares, Results p.6), so the stock is about 3% under its issue price one year on. On the headline basis, market cap over twice the H1 PAT is about 36x. On Ind AS H1 EPS of ₹1.07 (Results p.3) it is about 290x annualised (my arithmetic). Both are the same company, which is the point of the section above. Paid-up capital grew by 3.49 million shares in H1 (about 2.6%) through ESOP exercises at about ₹49 per share on average (₹171.75 million raised, Results p.5).

#### What is not said
- No FY27 or FY28 guidance for desks, occupancy or margin beyond "on track to unlock operating leverage" (Letter p.4).
- No split of the IGAAP/Ind AS gap by cause (escalation, rent-free periods, brokerage).
- No definition of the 3.0x multiple, and no breakdown of what the working-capital inflow is.
- The call recording was uploaded (idx 170), but no transcript yet. The transcript, when filed, is where any guidance and the Q&A on occupancy will show. I have not listened to the recording.

#### Cross-links to companies on file
- Embassy Developments is on the price list. The shareholders' letter says WeWork India is "promoted by the Embassy Group" (Letter p.9). Nothing in these filings says Embassy Developments is a landlord or related party of Wework India, so treat it only as a name to check.
- Theme: office leasing and capability centres. Flex took 24% of Q3 CY2026 leasing (about 5.0 msf of 21.0 msf, Presentation p.8). GCCs are 40.8% of WeWork's core revenue. Any on-file name that sells to GCCs or US-linked IT services would read the same demand signal. I have not tied this to a specific company.

### Sky Gold & Diamonds: record H1, but the cash flow rests on payables and a ₹1,800 Cr raise follows

Sky Gold & Diamonds is not in COMPANIES MASTER, so there is no thesis to test. This is an explanation of what the six filings say, what is odd inside them, and who it matters to. Items 127, 128 and 129 are the same 16-page PDF filed under three headings (identical text). It holds the board outcome, the QIP annexure, the auditor review reports and the full results. I read it once and cite it as "results PDF".

Price context: ₹935 (screener.in, 28-Oct-2026 06:13 IST). 52-week range ₹285 to ₹1,084. Market cap ₹14,483 Cr. The stock is 14% below its high and 3.3x its low (my arithmetic).

**What was filed**
- Q2 FY27 consolidated: revenue ₹2,390.7 Cr (up 61.0% YoY, up 18.8% QoQ), EBITDA ₹189.8 Cr (7.94%), PAT ₹129.4 Cr (up 93.2% YoY) (press release, p.2) [FILED].
- H1 FY27: revenue ₹4,403.5 Cr, EBITDA ₹346.5 Cr, PAT ₹234.3 Cr (up 111.9% YoY). The press headline "PAT up 111.9%" is the half-year figure, not the quarter's 93.2%.
- Board approved raising up to ₹1,800 Cr by QIP or other modes. EGM on 18-Nov-2026, 11:00 AM by video; cut-off 11-Nov; remote voting 15 to 17 Nov (results PDF p.1-2; EGM notice p.2, p.18).
- NRC granted 3,480 ESOP options at ₹10, vesting after at least one year (ESOP intimation p.3) [FILED].
- Purvi Gems & Jewellery: 100% acquired for ₹9 Cr cash, completed 7-Oct-2026 (press release p.3).
- A fraud at a subsidiary: ₹735.49 lakh loss booked as an exceptional item (results PDF p.16, note 6).

**1. The cash flow is better than guided, but payables paid for it**

Management says H1 operating cash flow was ₹136.7 Cr, which is 39% of EBITDA against earlier guidance of 20% (press release p.3) [MGMT]. The cash flow statement agrees: ₹13,666 lakh (results PDF p.15, text layer is OCR-quality, so I cross-checked each line against the balance sheet).

The balance sheet shows where it came from (p.14, my arithmetic, not filed):
- Trade payables rose from about ₹92 Cr to about ₹437 Cr, an increase of about ₹345 Cr. The cash flow line says 34,527.75 lakh, which matches.
- Inventories rose from ₹786 Cr to ₹1,003 Cr, up about ₹217 Cr.
- Trade receivables rose from ₹563 Cr to ₹854 Cr, up about ₹291 Cr.
- Without the payables increase, operating cash flow would be about minus ₹209 Cr (136.7 less 345.3).
- On a standalone basis operating cash flow was only ₹22.1 Cr (standalone cash flow, p.9). The other ₹114 Cr sits in the subsidiaries.

So the 39% conversion is a stretch in supplier credit, not a release of working capital. The filing does not say who the payables are owed to. In B2B jewellery the likely candidates are bullion or gold-on-lease suppliers [INFERENCE]. Whether that credit rolls or reverses is the thing to watch. If payables fall back to March levels, about ₹345 Cr of cash goes out.

Other balance sheet movements the filing does not explain:
- Other current financial assets went from ₹18.8 Cr to ₹382.3 Cr. On the standalone sheet the same line rose about ₹294 Cr while bank balances fell about ₹152 Cr, and net fixed-deposit placements in the cash flow were about ₹147 Cr. That looks like deposits or margin money being reclassified [INFERENCE]. Not confirmed.
- Gross borrowings are about ₹939 Cr against about ₹848 Cr in March (p.14, my arithmetic, OCR-read). Cash and bank balances total about ₹65 Cr.

**2. The ₹1,800 Cr raise: sizing against what is on the page**

- At ₹935, ₹1,800 Cr is 12.4% of market cap. With a 5% QIP discount (the notice allows up to 5%, EGM notice p.3) the issue price would be about ₹888, so roughly 2.03 Cr new shares, 13.1% of the 15.49 Cr existing shares, or 11.6% of the enlarged base (my arithmetic).
- It is 127% of consolidated book equity of ₹1,423 Cr, and 1.9x gross borrowings. It is about 3.8x the annualised H1 PAT (₹234.3 Cr x 2 = ₹469 Cr).
- The resolution is an enabling one. It lets the board raise equity, convertibles, preference shares, debt, GDRs, or a rights or preferential issue, in tranches, within 365 days (EGM notice p.2-3). It is not a commitment to raise the full ₹1,800 Cr at once.
- Uses listed (explanatory statement, p.18): working capital, repaying or refinancing debt, investing in subsidiaries, capex, acquisitions, contingencies, and "general corporate purposes". No split and no amount per head. The Reg 30 annexure also gives no use of funds (results PDF p.3-4).
- Promoters, directors and KMP will not subscribe to a QIP (EGM notice p.19). Their post-issue holding will therefore fall. The promoter holding itself is NOT FOUND in these files.
- A monitoring agency will track proceeds quarterly (EGM notice p.4).

Two readings:
- Bull. The business is working-capital hungry and growing 60% a year. Working capital net of payables is about ₹1,419 Cr (inventory 1,003 + receivables 854 - payables 437), about 14.8% of annualised Q2 revenue of ₹9,563 Cr. In March it was about 20% of FY26 revenue (₹6,295 Cr), and the fall is entirely the payables stretch. If revenue doubles and payables normalise, the funding need is of this order. A raise at a stock near ₹935 de-risks that and cuts the finance cost (₹25.5 Cr in Q2, up 45% YoY against revenue up 61%).
- Bear. The company has just reported a record half and still needs capital equal to 12% of its market value. The use list is generic. The raise lands after the stock tripled from ₹285. Prior payables stretch hides how much of the growth is funded by suppliers. Equity at this scale usually follows a peak print.

The one observation that separates them: the placement document after 18-Nov. If more than half the proceeds are earmarked for debt repayment and refinancing, it is balance-sheet repair after a payables-funded half. If more than half is working capital for named growth, it is the bull case. Separately, the Q3 balance sheet: payables below about ₹350 Cr with receivables flat would show the stretch unwinding.

**3. The fraud at Starmangalsutra**

Note 6 (results PDF p.16): in Q2 a wholly owned subsidiary, Starmangalsutra Private Limited, suffered unauthorised transfers of up to ₹1,070 lakh. A company-issued phone and laptop of an employee were compromised, with "deep-fake" profile cloning and coercion. A cyber police complaint is filed. ₹334.51 lakh was recovered (31%). The balance ₹735.49 lakh is an exceptional item (₹7.35 Cr, about 4% of Q2 PBT before exceptions of ₹173.4 Cr).

It does not move the numbers. It does matter for control quality:
- A subsidiary employee could move ₹10.7 Cr out by one compromised device.
- The note does not say whether there is insurance, whether a dual-approval control failed, or whether it was disclosed to the exchanges earlier. I did not find an earlier filing in this run's files. Check whether a Reg 30 fraud intimation exists.
- The auditor's review report has no emphasis of matter on it (results PDF p.11-12).

**4. Auditor and subsidiary coverage**

- The consolidated review report (p.11-12) says the comparatives for Q2 FY26 and the full FY26 audited accounts were reviewed or audited by a "predecessor auditor" (reports dated 13-Nov-2025 and 27-May-2026). So the statutory auditor changed after the FY26 audit. MSKA & Associates LLP is now the signing firm. The reason for the change is not in these filings.
- Two subsidiaries were reviewed by other auditors: revenue ₹496 Cr in Q2 and ₹871 Cr in H1, PAT ₹27.5 Cr and ₹49.9 Cr (p.12). That is about 20% of consolidated revenue and about 21% of PAT that this auditor relied on others for.
- The conclusion is unmodified on both standalone and consolidated.

**5. Internal inconsistencies in the press release**

- "Operational PBT" is defined as reported PBT "less non operational income". The numbers do not follow the definition. Q2 operational PBT of ₹173.4 Cr is exactly the PBT before exceptions (₹173.38 Cr), so it adds back the fraud loss and does not strip other income. Other income was ₹14.4 Cr in Q2 (up from ₹8.0 Cr in Q1). If other income were removed as the definition says, Q2 PBT would be about ₹159 Cr (my arithmetic).
- Q2 "operational PAT" is ₹138.56 Cr, ₹9.2 Cr above reported PAT, but the fraud add-back is ₹7.35 Cr before tax. For H1, operational PAT is ₹234.5 Cr, only ₹0.2 Cr above reported. Q1 ₹104.9 Cr plus Q2 ₹138.56 Cr is ₹243.5 Cr, not ₹234.5 Cr. One of the two operational PAT figures is wrong.
- The cash flow line reads "compared with (₹100.9) crore in H1 FY27". It should say H1 FY26 (the statement shows (100.87) Cr).
- The cover letter to the EGM notice says the e-voting entitlement cut-off is Wednesday 18-Nov. The board outcome and the notice say 11-Nov. The 18th is the meeting date.
- The ESOP intimation still refers to "Sky Gold Limited" after the name change to Sky Gold and Diamonds.

**6. What the filings do not say**
- Volume. Revenue includes gold value, so a 61% rise mixes tonnage with the gold price. There is no kilograms figure, no gold price assumption, no split of studded versus plain.
- Customer concentration and the top-customer receivable. Receivables of ₹854 Cr are about 35 days of H1 revenue, which is reasonable, but who owes it is not stated.
- Purvi Gems: turnover, profit and strategic reason are not disclosed. At ₹9 Cr it is immaterial to a ₹4,403 Cr revenue base.
- Debt cost, covenants, and the share of borrowings that are gold-metal loans.

**7. Smaller items**
- ESOP grant: 3,480 options at ₹10 is 0.002% of the share count (15.49 Cr shares). Intrinsic value at ₹935 is about ₹32 lakh (my arithmetic). Irrelevant in size. The NRC meeting ran three minutes (11:45 to 11:48). H1 ESOP expense is ₹1.66 Cr against ₹0.57 Cr a year ago.
- Standalone Q2: revenue ₹1,824.6 Cr (up 58.7% YoY), PAT ₹85.4 Cr (p.7). Consolidated adds about ₹566 Cr revenue and a Q2 PBT before exceptions of ₹173.4 Cr against standalone ₹114.1 Cr, so subsidiaries carry a large share of profit.
- Goodwill is ₹250.5 Cr, 17.6% of equity, unchanged. No impairment commentary.

**Who it matters to**
Holders or buyers ahead of the 18-Nov EGM, and anyone modelling a QIP overhang. The print is strong. The cash flow quality, the fraud control gap and the size of the raise are the three things to resolve before reading the result at face value.

**Cross-link:** No other gold or jewellery name is on file in COMPANIES MASTER. P N Gadgil Jewellers appears in this same run (N06) and is also not in the master. No company on file shares the QIP or fraud theme.

### Knack Packaging: 52% revenue growth, but core margin fell and treasury income carried profit

Knack Packaging is not in COMPANIES MASTER, so there is no thesis to test. This is an explanation of the filings, with the odd points marked and one cross-link. Four items were filed on 27-Oct: the investor presentation (36 pages), the press release, the results (21 pages) and a board outcome. Item 58 is byte-for-byte the same file as item 57 (same 21 pages, including the press release and the IPO-use statement). Treat it as one filing sent twice. The Screener summary for item 58 says "FY26", but the PDF says the quarter ended 30-Sep-2026. [FILED]

**What Knack is.** Ahmedabad maker of printed and laminated woven polypropylene (PLWPP) bags, mostly for feed, pet food, grains and fertiliser, with exports to 75+ countries. It listed on 8-Jul-2026 at ₹170 a share (results, p.8 note 7). The fresh issue raised ₹3,800 Mn; selling shareholders sold a further ₹595 Mn. [FILED]

**The numbers, consolidated, ₹ Mn (press release p.2; results p.12)** [FILED]

| | Q2 FY27 | Q1 FY27 | Q2 FY26 | YoY | QoQ |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Revenue from operations | 2,947.57 | 2,624.59 | 1,932.57 | +52.5% | +12.3% |
| Other income | 97.04 | 23.12 | 62.91 | +54% | +320% |
| EBITDA as filed (includes other income) | 497.65 | 591.73 | 383.04 | +29.9% | -15.9% |
| PAT | 250.20 | 305.28 | 206.36 | +21.2% | -18.0% |
| EPS (₹) | 2.07 | 3.05 | 2.06 | +0.5% | -32% |

My arithmetic, not filed, from the P&L on presentation p.31 and results p.12:

- **EBITDA without other income.** Q2 was ₹400.6 Mn, a 13.6% margin on revenue from operations. Q1 was ₹568.6 Mn (21.7%). Q2 FY26 was ₹320.1 Mn (16.6%). So core EBITDA is up 25% on last year but down 30% on Q1.
- **Gross margin** (revenue less materials, stock purchases and inventory change). Q2 was 34.6%, Q1 42.1%, Q2 FY26 41.2%. It fell about 7 points in one quarter. The company blames polymer and freight cost (press release p.2). The numbers fit that story. Other expenses also rose from ₹383 Mn to ₹465 Mn (+21%) on revenue up 12%.
- **Other income did the lifting.** It was ₹97 Mn, 27% of pre-JV profit (₹356.7 Mn). Core pre-tax profit before JV and without other income was ₹259.7 Mn, up 19% on last year and down 40% on Q1. The IPO cash is in fixed deposits (₹2,643 Mn). H1 interest income in the cash flow is only ₹61.3 Mn against H1 other income of ₹120.2 Mn (cash flow p.34). The other ₹58.8 Mn is not broken out in any filing. NOT FOUND.
- **The EBITDA margin basis is misstated.** The press release footnote says margin is EBITDA divided by revenue from operations. It is not. 497.65 / 3,044.61 (total income) = 16.35%, which is the printed figure. On the stated basis it would be 16.9%. The same holds for Q1 (22.35% printed, 22.55% on the stated basis) and for PAT margin (8.22% printed, 8.49% on the stated basis). The deck's "Revenue" row on p.27 is also total income (₹3,044.61 Mn), and the text beside it applies the 52.52% growth, which belongs to ₹2,947.57 Mn. On ₹3,044.61 Mn the growth is 52.6%. Small, but it is a pattern of loose labels.
- **EPS is flat.** PAT is up 21% but shares rose from 100 Mn to 122.4 Mn (+22.4%) after the IPO, so EPS is ₹2.07 against ₹2.06. The deck says net worth "fully absorbed" the dilution (p.26). That is true only in the sense that EPS held; it did not grow.
- **Depreciation** is ₹102.2 Mn against ₹71.9 Mn a year ago (+42%), ahead of revenue growth only in the sense that capacity was added in 2025-26. Finance cost is ₹38.7 Mn a quarter (about 8% a year on ₹1,911 Mn of borrowings).

**Cash and balance sheet (results pp.13-14; deck pp.32-35)** [FILED]

- **Cash from operations H1 was ₹220.6 Mn** against PAT ₹555.5 Mn, about 40%. Last year it was ₹389.4 Mn. Inventory (+₹269 Mn), receivables and other current assets (-₹557 Mn together in the cash flow) and tax paid (₹188 Mn against ₹99 Mn) took it. Capex was ₹275 Mn, so free cash flow is about -₹54 Mn (my arithmetic). Receivable days do not look worse (about 56-59 days on current revenue against 61 at March), so this is growth working capital, not a collection problem. It is still a quarter where the 52% growth ate cash.
- **IPO money mostly sits in deposits.** Cash ₹680 Mn plus other bank balances ₹2,647 Mn is ₹3,328 Mn. Borrowings are ₹1,911 Mn, almost unchanged from ₹1,925 Mn at March. Net cash is about ₹1,417 Mn (₹142 Cr), my arithmetic. The company holds ₹264 Cr in deposits while paying about 8% on ₹191 Cr of working-capital debt. The IPO objects (capex and general corporate purposes) did not include debt repayment, so this may be by design. Still, the deck calls the debt-to-equity fall to 0.26x "zero leverage dependency". That is arithmetic from the cash, not a change in how the business is funded.
- **Use of IPO proceeds (results p.9 note 9; Annexure C p.20)**: ₹3,200 Mn is earmarked for the new Borisana plant, and only ₹7.04 Mn (0.2%) has been spent. General corporate purposes: ₹294.69 Mn of ₹311.49 Mn (95%) used in one quarter. Issue expenses: ₹259.03 Mn of ₹288.51 Mn. Unutilised: ₹3,239.24 Mn. The board and the monitoring agency (CARE Ratings) report no deviation. What it does NOT say: what the ₹294.69 Mn of general corporate use was spent on. The head is reported as one number. NOT FOUND. The deck says the new facility will be commissioned by October 2027 (p.19), so the capex head is a year-long build still to be paid for.
- **Inconsistency on capacity.** Deck p.28 says the Indian plants are "firing at full capacity" with the capex "fully absorbed". Deck p.12 shows Unit 4 (April 2026) and Unit 5 (July 2026) newly established, and p.19 says the new facility lifts capacity only by October 2027. If the plants are full, growth to October 2027 has to come from price, mix and the two new units.
- **"Revenue nearly doubling"** (deck p.30, in the debt note) is wrong. H1 revenue is up 46.8% on the press release, and 47.2% on the deck's total-income figure on p.28. The two documents give two H1 growth rates.

**The Mexico JV (results p.8 note 6; consolidated p.11).** [FILED]

- Sayem Knack S.A. de C.V. is 50% owned. Group share of loss: ₹26.15 Mn in Q2, ₹34.45 Mn in Q1, ₹60.60 Mn in H1. It is equity-accounted, so its revenue never appears in Knack's. The press release line "zero revenue offset today" therefore describes how the accounting works, not a gap that closes when the JV sells. The claim that PAT margin "recovers fully" at breakeven has no date and no breakeven volume behind it.
- The JV's carrying value in the consolidated books fell from ₹34.55 Mn to nil (p.13). The loss booked (₹60.6 Mn) is bigger than the carrying value, by about ₹26 Mn (my arithmetic). So the extra loss is sitting against some other claim on the JV, probably a loan or advance. The investing cash flow shows "loans given net" of ₹138.3 Mn (₹124.5 Mn standalone), and other non-current assets rose by ₹91 Mn. That is inference. The note that lists the JV loans is NOT FOUND in these filings.
- The ownership changed on 30-Dec-2025: Sacos y Empaques Internacionales, described in the note as "a key strategic customer", and an individual, handed their 50% to Bessher Holding S.A.P.I. de C.V. The note still calls Sacos the key customer, and says nothing about who Bessher is. That is worth asking.
- The JV's results are reviewed only by another auditor, and Talati & Talati say they rely solely on that report (auditor review p.11). Same for the South African subsidiary (revenue ₹122.3 Mn, PAT ₹15.6 Mn in Q2). The review conclusion is unmodified. The Q2 FY26 and H1 FY26 comparatives were not reviewed at all (note 8), because the company was not listed then. So the "52.5% YoY" compares reviewed figures with unreviewed ones.

**The big order (deck p.16).** [MGMT] A renewal and new order from "one of the largest private corporations in the world" for pet food and animal nutrition packaging, including the USA. About 60 million bags a year, about ₹1,500 Mn a year, contract to December 2028. That is about ₹25 a bag, and about 13.5% of annualised H1 revenue (11,144 Mn), my arithmetic. The customer is not named on the page, but the growth slide (p.19) mentions "partnership with Cargill". The filing does not say how much is new and how much is a renewal of existing supply. Top-10 customers were 40.9% of FY26 revenue (p.6).

**Two readings.**

- *Bull:* Revenue is strong (+52% YoY, +12% QoQ). Gross margin fell on input cost. Management says price revisions will show "from Q3" (press release p.2). The cost shock is from crude-linked polymer and Red Sea freight, which are cyclical. A multi-year contract to 2028 gives volume visibility. The ₹3,239 Mn of unspent IPO money funds the build.
- *Bear:* The margin drop came while revenue grew 12% in a quarter, so it is a pricing-power question, not only a cost question. The wording is also inconsistent: the press release says price revisions are "currently underway" and take effect from Q3, while the CMD quote on p.3 says they were "implemented over past few months" and benefits come "over next few quarters". Profit growth rests partly on treasury income. Cash conversion is 40%. The JV is a cash drain with no stated end.
- *What separates them:* Q3 FY27 (results due about early Feb 2027). If consolidated EBITDA without other income, on revenue from operations, is back above about 17% (the H1 FY26 level was 18.4%, and H1 FY27 is 17.4%, so 17% means "no further erosion"), the cost shock is being passed on. If it stays near 13.6%, pricing power is the problem. That threshold is mine, not filed.

**Price context.** ₹209 (screener.in, 28-Oct-2026 06:11 IST). 52-week range ₹171 to ₹222, market cap ₹2,553 Cr. The stock is 23% above the ₹170 IPO price and 6% below the 52-week high. On H1 PAT annualised (₹1,111 Mn) and the current 122.4 Mn shares, that is about 23 times earnings; with net cash of about ₹142 Cr taken off, enterprise value is about ₹2,411 Cr, or about 12 times annualised H1 core EBITDA (₹1,938 Mn). All my arithmetic. The price is near the top of its short listing range, after a quarter where the core margin fell. That is the setup; there is no thesis here to say whether it matters.

**Odd points to keep.**

1. The margin footnote is wrong about its own basis (above).
2. The results outcome was digitally signed at 19:40 (results p.2), and the board meeting ended at 17:30. The listing rules ask for the outcome within 30 minutes of the meeting's close. The signature time is not the exchange receipt time. The exchange timestamp should be checked, but I could not.
3. The deck title slide reads "Earnings Call", but no call date appears in any of the four filings.
4. Q1 FY27 gross margin looks flattered by a ₹316 Mn finished-goods build, which was almost nil in Q2 (change in inventories -₹2.7 Mn). Part of the Q1-to-Q2 margin drop is the cost of goods sold catching up to the stock built earlier. [INFERENCE]

**Cross-link.** I found no company on file in packaging or polymer. Mold-Tek Packaging (not in master, in N07: Q2 board on 2-Nov) is the nearest listed peer by theme; its print is the first cross-check on polymer and freight pass-through. Both are read-across only, not filed links. [INFERENCE]

### Gallantt Ispat: shutdown quarter, EBITDA down 40%, and a ₹3,000 Cr plan with no funding map

Gallantt Ispat is not in COMPANIES MASTER, so there is no thesis to test. It makes TMT bars and sponge iron at Gorakhpur (Uttar Pradesh) and Kutch (Gujarat). Three items: a promoter-group gift of shares with no summary (item 37), the Q2 earnings call transcript (item 38) and the SAST disclosure PDF (item 39). Items 37 and 39 are the same event. The first is the exchange headline, the second is the paper. [FILED]

**The earnings call transcript (13 pages).**

*Filing oddities first.* The cover letter says the call was held "Wednesday, October 27, 2026" and the subject line says the half-year ended "30th September, 2025" (p.1). The transcript itself is headed "October 21, 2026" (p.2). 21-Oct-2026 is a Wednesday; 27-Oct is a Tuesday. So the call was on 21-Oct and the cover letter has the wrong date and year. The filing was signed 27-Oct at 15:52, still inside five working days of the call. The CFO is listed as "Chief Accounts Officer" on the cover page of the transcript (p.2) but introduced as CFO in the speech (p.3 and p.6). The results themselves were filed before the call and are not in this batch.

*Numbers [MGMT], stated by Amit Jalan on pp.6-7:*

| ₹ Cr | Q2 FY27 | Q1 FY27 | Q2 FY26 |
|---|---|---|---|
| Revenue | 1,146 | 1,146 | 1,013 |
| EBITDA | 111 | 185 | 135 |
| EBITDA margin | 9.64% | 16.19% | 12.91% |
| EBITDA per tonne (₹) | 6,150 | 8,010 | not given |
| PAT | 61 | 124 | 89 |
| EPS (₹) | 2.51 | not given | not given |

H1 FY27: revenue ₹2,292 Cr (+7%), EBITDA ₹296 Cr (12.91%, against ₹382 Cr and 17.85%), PAT ₹184 Cr against ₹263 Cr (-30%), EPS ₹7.63.

*Things that do not add up, my arithmetic, not filed:*

- **The Q2 FY26 margin is off.** ₹135 Cr on ₹1,013 Cr is 13.3%, not 12.91%. The 12.91% printed for Q2 FY26 equals this year's H1 margin (296 / 2,292). Probably a slip in the script.
- **Volume against revenue.** TMT sales fell from 1,92,000 t in Q1 to 1,65,000 t in Q2, down 14% (p.5). EBITDA per tonne of ₹6,150 and ₹8,010 imply total volume of about 1.80 lakh t in Q2 and 2.31 lakh t in Q1, down 22%. Revenue was flat. So realisation per tonne rose by roughly 16% to 28% in one quarter. But the CEO says the price rise "happened only in the last one month, in September" and that realisation "not making any impact in Q2 broadly" (p.8). Both cannot be true. Either mix (more value-added product, less low-priced intermediate) did the work, or the tonnage base behind "EBITDA per tonne" is not what it seems. The call does not resolve it.
- **Does EBITDA include other income?** Not stated. Other income was ₹18 Cr. If it is inside the ₹111 Cr, core EBITDA is about ₹93 Cr, an 8.1% margin.
- **"One-time" shutdowns.** Management calls them "all one-time" (p.4). But there were two (a planned Gorakhpur shutdown and an unplanned power-plant extension caused by a turbine fault). Another shutdown is coming: at Gujarat, "once we synchronize our sponge unit in Q4, there would be a shutdown required" (p.12). So the shutdown effect is not behind them on management's own words.

*Capex and funding, the largest unanswered point.*

- Capex programme ₹3,000 Cr, ₹650 Cr spent so far (p.7). That is 22%. H1 capex ₹278 Cr (₹141 Cr in Q2). Capital work in progress rose from ₹349 Cr to ₹598 Cr.
- Balance for FY27, the CFO says: "around ₹500 crores" (p.9). Then he splits it: ₹400 Cr Gorakhpur, ₹240 Cr mines, ₹170 Cr solar, ₹100 Cr Kutch. Those four add up to ₹910 Cr, not ₹500 Cr. Either the ₹500 Cr figure or the split is wrong, or the split is programme totals. The call does not say which.
- Left after FY27: ₹3,000 Cr less ₹650 Cr is ₹2,350 Cr, of which ₹500 Cr (or ₹910 Cr) falls in this year. H1 EBITDA annualises to about ₹592 Cr, so the remaining programme equals about four years of current EBITDA before interest, tax and dividend. "Funded through internal accruals" and "no term loans" (pp.4, 6) is a claim that depends on EBITDA recovering strongly.
- A **larger greenfield plan** built around the coal and iron ore blocks will be shown in "Quarter 4 '27" (p.4). Asked about funding, the Vice Chairman says "we will decide the capital restructuring" (p.7). That is not a promise of internal funding. Debt or equity for a bigger project is open.
- Balance sheet (p.6): net worth ₹3,487 Cr; borrowings ₹592 Cr, mostly working capital; ₹426 Cr in bank balances and liquid investments plus ₹266 Cr treasury deployment. Net cash about ₹100 Cr (692 less 592, my arithmetic). Inventory ₹669 Cr against ₹551 Cr in March, +₹118 Cr, which management ties to the shutdown and to stock built for the new capacity (p.9).

*Capacity and operations [MGMT].*

- Gorakhpur expansion: management says "1 to 1.23 million tonnes" on p.4 and "1.3 million" on p.5. The capacity is said to start about 10-Nov and reach 85-90% by December end (p.11). So Q3 gets only a partial benefit and Q4 the full one.
- The 18 MW solar plant at Siddhpur (p.5) becomes "22 megawatts in Gujarat" in a later answer (p.9). The 60 MW Prayagraj solar plant is due in Q4 FY27. Together they are said to save ₹30-40 Cr a year (p.5).
- Kutch rolling mill utilisation 68% against 66% (p.5, p.11). Management says "not much result" in Q2.
- The power plant was to restart "within a week" of 21-Oct (p.5).
- Brand premium over unbranded TMT: 5-6% in UP, 3-4% in Gujarat (p.10).
- Pricing: TMT prices in September were about 21% above a year ago (p.5). Raw material cost rose about ₹3,000 a tonne in Q2 against a finished price rise of about 20% (p.8). That is the whole bull case in one line, and it is a statement of direction, not a number for Q2.

*Coal block: the statements disagree.* The Vice Chairman says Gallantt "was awarded a coal block in Singrauli in August 2026" (p.4). The CEO says the block "was just allotted last month" and that "we have another 1-month time to decide if to conclude our due diligence and move forward with it" (p.11). So the block is not yet accepted, and the award date differs by a month between two speakers. Operations would take "2 to 3 years" after the decision. The pitch that Gallantt now "holds both of the key raw materials" (p.4) runs ahead of this. The iron ore blocks are still in exploration with clearances pending, "FY28 remained our target" (p.5). The ₹2,000 a tonne EBITDA benefit from the mines would build over "a year or 2 post FY28" (p.8).

**Two readings.**

- *Bull:* Q2 was hit by two shutdowns. Prices are up about 20% against costs up about ₹3,000 a tonne. Capacity is up about 23% from November. Solar and mines lower cost from FY27-FY28. The spread is the largest driver, and it arrives in Q3.
- *Bear:* The "one-time" shutdowns recur (Gujarat in Q4). Utilisation at Kutch is 68%. Capex is large, the funding story is open, and the coal block is optional and unsettled. The earnings recovery depends on a TMT price rise that is only one month old.
- *What separates them:* Q3 FY27 (results due about early-to-mid Feb 2027). EBITDA per tonne at or above about ₹8,000 (Q1 FY27) on TMT sales of at least about 1,92,000 t would show a recovered base before any mine benefit. If per-tonne EBITDA stays near ₹6,000, the shutdown story is not the whole story. The thresholds are my choices from the Q1 numbers.

**Price context.** ₹542 (screener.in, 28-Oct-2026 06:11 IST). 52-week range ₹471 to ₹948, market cap ₹13,071 Cr. The stock is 43% below its high and 15% above the low. On H1 PAT annualised (₹368 Cr), that is about 36 times earnings. With net cash of about ₹100 Cr taken off, EV is about ₹12,970 Cr, or about 22 times annualised H1 EBITDA, and about 17.5 times Q1's run rate (₹740 Cr). All my arithmetic. Even on the better quarter, the price asks for the capacity, the solar and the mines to deliver.

**The promoter-group gift (items 37 and 39).** [FILED]

- Smriti Agrawal, a promoter-group shareholder, received 5,00,000 shares (0.207%) by gift on 27-Oct-2026 (cover letter, image p.1; Form C, p.12). Mode: off-market gift. Value: "N.A.". The seller named in the second form is **Ashwin Gupta, a public-category shareholder** (image p.6). At ₹542, the shares are worth about ₹27 Cr (my arithmetic). The filing does not state the relationship between donor and recipient.
- **Promoter group total:** 17,78,18,586 shares (73.698%) before, 17,83,18,586 (73.905%) after (Annexure 1, image pages 10-11). Gallantt Trust alone holds 60.051%. Dinesh R Agarwal holds 5.807%, CP Agrawal Daughters Trust 2.072%, Smriti 4.849% going up to 5.056%. Total shares: 24,12,80,945.
- **Public float.** A gift from a public holder to the promoter group moves shares from public to promoter. Public holding falls to 26.095%. The 75% ceiling on promoter holding leaves room of 1.095%, about 26.4 lakh shares (my arithmetic). One more transfer like this is small, but the headroom is not large if the group were to buy more.
- **The filing contains two forms, and the first is wrong.** Pages 2-5 report "holding of acquirer along with PACs" of 1,17,00,000 shares (4.849%) before and 1,22,00,000 (5.056%) after. That is Smriti's own holding only, labelled as the whole concert group. The second set (pages 6-12) gives the full table and names the seller. The first set names no seller. The 5.056% line also crosses 5%, which looks like the reason the disclosure was made at all, since a change of 0.207% is under the usual 2% test. That reading is inference. The Annexure and Form C are consistent with each other.
- No PAN is shown ("provided without PAN details"). Whether a prior notice for the inter-se gift was filed is NOT FOUND in this batch.
- The CEO is Mayank Agarwal (Agrawal in one place). He is on the PAC list with nil shares. Smriti Agrawal shares his surname; any relationship is not stated, so I do not claim one.

**Cross-links.** Sambhv Steel is on file (WATCHLIST / BUY ON DIPS, results and call on 30-Oct, in T10). It makes sponge iron, billets and TMT-type long products as far as I know, so Gallantt's reported 21% TMT price rise, coal cost up 4.5-5% of sales, and sponge-iron and pig-iron price jump in the late part of Q2 are the read-across for its 30-Oct call. [INFERENCE] Transrail Light (on file) buys steel for towers; flat steel and long steel prices differ, so it is only a mood read-across. [INFERENCE]

### Dynamic Cables: revenue up 29%, profit up 13%, and raw material cost is the gap

Dynamic Cables is not in COMPANIES MASTER, so there is no thesis to test. These are standalone results (the company has no subsidiary in the statements). I read all five items.

Price context: ₹338 (screener.in, 28-Oct-2026 06:10 IST). 52-week range ₹237 to ₹560. Market cap ₹1,639 Cr. The stock is 40% below its high (my arithmetic). The ESOP exercise price is ₹324, so the stock sits just above the option strike.

**The numbers** (press release p.2-3; results p.4) [FILED]
- Q2 FY27 revenue ₹363.2 Cr, up 29% YoY and up 4.0% QoQ (Q1 ₹349.1 Cr).
- Operating profit ₹36.3 Cr, margin 10.0%. That is down from 10.9% in Q1 and 11.0% in Q2 FY26.
- PAT ₹22.15 Cr, up 13% YoY but down 11.2% QoQ (Q1 ₹24.95 Cr). EPS ₹4.57.
- H1 revenue ₹712.3 Cr (up 31%), operating profit ₹74.3 Cr (up 29%), PAT ₹47.1 Cr (up 24%), EPS ₹9.72.
- Order book about ₹829 Cr. Export sales about ₹56 Cr in H1 (7.9% of H1 revenue). Receivable days about 59 (on trailing six-month gross sales) [MGMT].

**Why profit lagged revenue** (my arithmetic from the Q2, Q1 and Q2 FY26 columns of the results, p.4)

Raw material consumed plus the inventory change, as a share of revenue:
- Q2 FY27: 83.3%. Q1 FY27: 80.6%. Q2 FY26: 79.3%.
- That is 270 bps worse QoQ and 410 bps worse YoY.

Two lines held operating margin near 10%:
- Employee cost fell from 4.4% of revenue to 3.6% YoY.
- Other expenses fell from 5.4% to 3.1% YoY, and from ₹15.9 Cr in Q1 to ₹11.2 Cr in Q2 (a drop of ₹4.7 Cr on higher revenue).
- Net: material cost cost about 4.1 points, the two overheads gave back about 3.1, leaving the 1.0 point margin loss.

The filing does not say why other expenses dropped ₹4.7 Cr QoQ. Other expenses fell from 4.6% to 3.1% of revenue QoQ. Without that drop, the QoQ margin loss would have been about 2.4 points instead of 0.9 (my arithmetic). So the margin is being held by an overhead line that may not repeat. The material cost rise is the real driver. The filing gives no explanation (metal price pass-through lag, product mix toward government tenders, or export pricing). The MD's comment mentions "crude price volatility" only (press release p.2) [MGMT].

Finance cost rose to ₹4.2 Cr from ₹2.9 Cr in Q1 (up 45% QoQ) while on-balance-sheet debt fell from ₹71.2 Cr to ₹37.3 Cr. The filing does not explain the gap. Average borrowing during the quarter may have been higher than the closing figure, or LC charges may sit in the line [INFERENCE].

**Balance sheet and debt** (press release p.3)
- On-balance-sheet debt ₹37.3 Cr. LC creditors (off balance sheet) ₹77.7 Cr, up from ₹64.6 Cr in June. Total borrowing ₹115.0 Cr. Cash and equivalents ₹33.2 Cr. Net debt ₹81.8 Cr, against ₹58.2 Cr in March (up ₹23.6 Cr in H1, my arithmetic) and ₹88.7 Cr in June (down ₹6.9 Cr in Q2).
- 68% of "total borrowing" is LC creditors, which do not appear as borrowings in the statutory balance sheet. Net debt to H1 operating profit annualised (₹148.6 Cr) is 0.55x, modest.
- Inventory rose from about ₹173 Cr in March to about ₹243 Cr, up 41% (balance sheet p.3, cash flow shows (7,064.70) lakh, which ties). Revenue in H1 was ₹712 Cr against ₹654 Cr in H2 FY26 (1,197.8 less 543.6), up 9%. So inventory outgrew sales. Receivables did not grow in H1.
- Receivable days of 59 only reconcile if sales are taken gross of 18% GST. Receivables of about ₹274 Cr on net H1 revenue are about 70 days. Both are consistent, but the 59 figure is on a flattering basis (my arithmetic).

**Growth and the new plant**
- The MD says the new plant "will start contributing from second half" and machinery is installed (press release p.2-3) [MGMT]. No capacity, no capex amount, no revenue potential is given. This is the key unknown.
- The preferential issue of ₹96.6 Cr (June 2024) is fully used "including advances" (note 6, p.4). Nothing left to fund the plant from that source, so the new plant is funded by internal cash and bank lines.
- Order book ₹829 Cr is 0.58 years of annualised H1 revenue (my arithmetic). It is a reasonable, not a long, runway. No prior-quarter figure is on file to show direction.

**Two readings**
- Bull. Revenue is up 29% with the plant not yet live. Exports and private-sector demand add to the government base. EPS is ₹9.72 for H1, so at ₹338 the stock trades near 17x annualised H1 EPS (₹19.4). The plant adds volume from H2.
- Bear. Revenue grew 29% and PAT 13%. Material cost is eating margin, the overhead offset may not repeat, and working capital absorbed cash (inventory up 41% in six months, net debt up). Another plant raises depreciation and finance cost before it raises profit.

The single observation that separates them: Q3 operating margin. If it recovers to 10.5% or better on lower other expenses than Q2, the Q2 dip was timing. If it stays near 10.0% or lower, with other expenses back above ₹14 Cr a quarter, the margin is structurally lower. The Q3 results (due around late January) settle it.

**Small items**
- BSE fine: ₹2,000 plus GST, total ₹2,360, for filing the June-quarter shareholding pattern one day late (board comments, p.1; BSE mail p.4-5). The board says it misread whether the shareholding pattern falls under the integrated filing framework. The BSE letter warns that a second consecutive year of delay under Regulation 31 moves the company to Z group and brings suspension risk. It also demands payment within 15 days, which would be about 29-Aug-2026. The board note is dated 27-Oct, 74 days after the BSE letter. The filing does not confirm the fine was paid. The amount is trivial. The pattern of an avoidable miss is the point.
- ESOP: 183,405 options outstanding at ₹324, about 0.4% of the 4.85 Cr shares (note 7, p.4; my arithmetic).
- Labour codes: no material impact assessed (note 8).
- The audio-recording filing (item 21) adds no content. I did not hear the call.
- The Screener one-line summary for item 23 reads "H1 revenue Rs 71.45 crore, profit Rs 37.84 crore". It is wrong by 10x and mixes years. 71.45 is total income in ₹ thousand-lakh form misread, and 37.84 is H1 FY26 PAT. The correct figures are ₹712 Cr revenue and ₹47.1 Cr PAT.

**Cross-link:** Transrail Lightning (T18) is on file and shares the state power-utility tender cycle that drives Dynamic's government order book. Its Q3 intake is a useful read for utility spend. Atlanta Electricals (T15) is on file in the same T&D equipment area. No direct competitor is on file. Dynamic's margin dip is a metal-cost and mix story, not a Transrail-style order-book story.

### 3i Infotech: tax disputes of ₹775 Cr settled, price paid not disclosed

Not in COMPANIES MASTER. Two filings.

**Item 2, the tax update (1 page) [FILED]**
- The company opted into the Direct Tax Vivad Se Vishwas Scheme, 2024 for four assessment years: 2012-13, 2013-14, 2014-15 and 2016-17. It first disclosed this on 28-May-2026 and again on 3-Sep-2026; those two filings are not in this file, so I cannot check the earlier wording.
- On 22-Oct the company asked the Commissioner of Income Tax (Appeals) to withdraw the pending appeals. On 26-Oct the CIT(A) treated them as withdrawn and dismissed them. The CIT(A) recorded no finding on the merits.
- The disputed additions and disallowances add up to about ₹77,505 lakh, which is ₹775.05 Cr.
- The company says the event happened on 26-Oct at 1:21 p.m. and the filing is stamped 27-Oct at 12:41. That is inside the 24-hour Reg 30 window by about 40 minutes (my arithmetic).

**What the figure means, and what is missing**
- ₹775 Cr is the disputed additions to income, not the tax. It is larger than the company's whole market cap of ₹419 Cr (screener.in, 28-Oct-2026 06:09 IST), about 1.85 times (my arithmetic). Tax on additions at a rate near 30% would be roughly ₹230 Cr, or over half the market cap. That is an illustration to show scale, not a filed number, and what is actually payable under the scheme can differ.
- The filing does NOT say: the amount paid or payable to settle, the date of payment, how it will be funded, whether the company had already provided for it, the profit and loss effect (write-back or charge) or in which quarter. It also does not say why assessment year 2015-16 is not in the list.
- The question for the earnings call: is settlement cash out, and is it larger or smaller than the provision on the balance sheet. A settlement below provision is a one-time gain; above is a one-time hit.

**Two readings**
- Bull: a decade-old overhang is gone, the uncertainty of a ₹775 Cr dispute against a ₹419 Cr company is removed, and cash cost is capped by the scheme.
- Bear: the company paid a large sum, possibly more than it had provided, and a small company is spending cash it may need.
- What separates them: the 2-Nov call and the Q2 notes. Look for the settlement amount and whether Q2 profit carries a one-off.

**Item 1, call notice [FILED]**
Q2 and H1 FY27 earnings call on Monday 2-Nov-2026 at 12:00 IST, with the Group CEO and CFO named. No results date is given in this notice. Routine.

**Price context**
₹20.2 (screener.in, 28-Oct-2026 06:09 IST), 52-week range ₹12.6 to ₹27.8, about 27% below the high and 60% above the low.

**Cross-links**
None to a name on file. Anyone holding another small IT-services name with old tax litigation should watch what 3i reports as the settlement cost.

### Mahindra Logistics: Rivigo turns EBITDA positive, but quarterly PAT is flat

Not in COMPANIES MASTER. This is the transcript of the 23-Oct call on Q2 FY27 (f4d389, 19 pages), filed 27-Oct. The numbers are management's. [MGMT]

**What was reported (pp.3-7).**
- Revenue ₹2,095 Cr, up 24% year on year (p.5). My arithmetic: Q2 FY26 about ₹1,690 Cr.
- EBITDA ₹124 Cr against ₹85 Cr; margin 5.9% against 5.0%. After removing Ind AS 116, margin is 2.9% against 1.8% (p.5). So about ₹61 Cr of "real" EBITDA (my arithmetic: 2.9% x 2,095).
- PAT ₹26 Cr against a ₹10 Cr loss a year ago and ₹25 Cr in Q1 (p.2). Revenue rose from Q1 to Q2 but PAT moved ₹1 Cr. Year-on-year is a big improvement. Quarter on quarter, nothing.
- Standalone other income includes a one-time ₹15 Cr dividend from a wholly owned subsidiary. It cancels at consolidation, so the ₹26 Cr is clean (p.5).
- H1 free cash flow ₹52 Cr against minus ₹210 Cr. ROCE 12.9% against 1.7%. Consolidated debt ₹55 Cr, cash ₹363 Cr, net cash ₹308 Cr (p.5).

**Segment arithmetic.** Revenue by segment: contract logistics ₹1,688 Cr, last-mile ₹68 Cr, freight forwarding ₹51 Cr, express (Rivigo, MESPL) ₹169 Cr, mobility ₹119 Cr. Sum ₹2,095 Cr, which ties to the consolidated figure (my arithmetic). EBITDA: 115 + 1.6 + 1.7 + 2.3 + 3.4 = ₹124 Cr, which also ties. Two points follow.
- Contract logistics is 93% of EBITDA (115 of 124). The group is a contract logistics company with four small side businesses. Express at ₹2.3 Cr is 1.9% of EBITDA on 8% of revenue.
- Express EBITDA margin is 1.4% (2.3 / 169). An analyst cited a peer at 10 to 12% (p.6). The CEO called it "a decade story" (p.7). Break-even is real but this is not yet an earnings driver.
- One inconsistency: the CEO says last-mile is "part of our contract logistics" (p.14), yet the five segments add exactly to the total with last-mile separate. Either the ₹1,688 Cr excludes last-mile or a line is missing. Minor.

**Margin test.**
- Contract logistics revenue +28%, gross margin in rupees +20% (p.3). So the gross margin percentage fell year on year. My arithmetic: at 10.5% now (analyst, p.11), last year was about 11.2% (10.5 x 1.28 / 1.20). Same pattern in mobility: revenue +33%, gross margin +19%.
- Management repeats a target of 100 bps gross margin improvement. The CFO then clarifies it is "over the medium term", not two quarters, not "within one year", measured from last year's exit (p.17). The CEO's line in the same call, "we should achieve this", sounds nearer. Current 10.5% against about 11% in FY26 (analyst's figure) means margin is below the exit level it is meant to improve from. [MGMT]
- Manpower costs are not passed through; there are no written contracts for them. The Provident Fund wage-ceiling change from ₹15,000 to ₹25,000 raises cost further (p.8). Fuel was passed through in full.
- Q3 festive-season flex-site costs "will play out again this year" (p.9). So Q3 margin has a known drag. [MGMT]

**Things said that change earlier messaging.**
- Mahindra & Mahindra is about 60% of revenue (58% in FY26). An analyst said management had guided it down. The CEO said there is "no internal target to reduce" M&M share and he wants "100%" of M&M's business (p.17-18). Concentration is a stated choice now.
- The whitespace (empty warehouse space) problem is declared solved at 5% of June-2025 levels, and management says it "will not be calling this out from now onwards" (p.3). A disclosure that used to be tracked is withdrawn.
- Last-mile moves from a "no-loss" stance to growth. Freight forwarding revenue is down 44% year on year (₹51 Cr against about ₹91 Cr, my arithmetic) and the CEO calls H2 a "build phase" with real results in FY28 (p.14).
- A new technology platform capex was approved by the board "today" (23-Oct). The amount is not given. Overall FY27 capex guidance is about ₹100 Cr plus or minus ₹10 Cr, half spent in H1 (p.12). Whether the new platform is inside that ₹110 Cr is not stated. NOT FOUND.
- Monsoon was 14% below the long period average. The CEO says "if one pedal slows down we will start pushing the other pedal" (p.11). That is a general promise, no numbers.

**Two readings.** Bull: Rivigo break-even, last-mile pivot, new-business wins running above a 3.5x internal target (p.13), cash-generative with net cash ₹308 Cr. Bear: quarterly PAT flat, gross margin below last year's level, 60% from one customer, and every non-core segment with a one-line EBITDA. The observation that separates them: Q3 contract logistics gross margin against 10.5% and Rivigo EBITDA against ₹2.3 Cr. A Q3 gross margin below 10.5% confirms the bear case. Date: Q3 results, around late January 2027.

Price ₹434 (screener.in, 28-Oct-2026 06:12 IST). 52-week range ₹275 to ₹451, so within 4% of the high. Market cap ₹4,311 Cr. On H1 PAT of about ₹51 Cr (₹25 Cr + ₹26 Cr, my arithmetic) annualised to ₹102 Cr, that is about 42 times earnings (my arithmetic). The stock is priced for the margin target being met.

Cross-link: Pranik Logistics is in this batch and shows the same lease-accounting gap (PBILDT before lease rentals). Mahindra's own adjusted EBITDA is roughly half its reported EBITDA. Both are listed in the logistics theme in this run's price list alongside Sical Logistics, whose master status I have not checked.

### Ador Welding: revenue up 23%, EBITDA up 9%, steel pass-through lagged

Not in COMPANIES MASTER. Transcript of the 23-Oct Q2 FY27 call (717aa7, 17 pages), filed 27-Oct. [MGMT] unless stated.

**Numbers (pp.3-4).**
- Q2 revenue ₹345 Cr, +23%. H1 revenue ₹654 Cr, +23%. My arithmetic: Q1 about ₹309 Cr, H1 FY26 about ₹532 Cr, Q2 FY26 about ₹280 Cr.
- Q2 gross margin 34.2%. Q2 EBITDA +9%. H1 EBITDA margin 11.2%, "slightly higher than last year" (about ₹73 Cr, my arithmetic).
- Revenue +23% with EBITDA +9% means the Q2 EBITDA margin was about 11% lower than last year in relative terms (my arithmetic: 1.09 / 1.23). The Q2 margin itself was not stated. Silence is information: management gave the H1 figure, which is flattered by a better Q1.
- Interim dividend ₹7.50 a share. On about 1.74 crore shares (my arithmetic: market cap / price) that is about ₹13 Cr.

**Why margin fell, in management's words.**
- About 80% of the gross margin dip is input inflation, 15 to 20% is "an aggressive growth strategy" (p.4).
- Steel rose 7 to 8% from Q1 to Q2 (p.13). Price rises were taken "in line" with steel but with a time lag, and in Q2 "a bit slow" (p.11). Passing it through is "the only way" to restore margin (p.13).
- Volume growth is mid-teens, so price and mix add roughly 7 to 8 points of the 23% (my arithmetic). Growth is about one-third pricing. That is the part that disappears if steel falls.
- Export mix was "slightly different to an ideal margin mix" but "margin accretive" (p.6). Management accepts lower margin for brand building.
- Target: EBITDA 12 to 13% and gross margin 36 to 38% (p.5). H1 is 11.2% and Q2 gross 34.2%, so Q2 is 180 to 380 bps short of the gross target. Recovery from Q3 is promised; "early days in Q3" (p.10). Steel is not softening (p.11).

**Balance sheet.**
- Short-term bank borrowing rose to about ₹28 Cr from ₹0.27 Cr at FY26 (p.16). Explanation: letters of credit and bank guarantees, plus strategic steel stock. Inventory is up and payables are down, 75 to 80% because of the order book and 15 to 20% because steel was scarce (p.8). The CEO says "I would not read too much into the H1 data" (p.16). Working-capital strain is real this quarter. The test is whether short-term debt returns to near zero by the March balance sheet.
- Capex has a contradiction. Early in the call: normal capex ₹35 to 40 Cr a year, rising to ₹60 to 70 Cr for one or two years (p.9). Later: "This year will be approximately ₹30 to 35 Cr", and ₹60 to 70 Cr "for the next 2 financial years, FY27, '28" (p.16). FY27 is the current year. Both cannot hold. Either FY27 is ₹30 to 35 Cr and FY28 and FY29 are higher, or the figures are loose. Ask.
- No debt or equity raise planned (p.9).

**Growth targets.**
- ₹2,000 Cr revenue by FY29: "not very far off track" (p.9). H1 annualised is about ₹1,308 Cr (my arithmetic). Getting to ₹2,000 Cr in FY29 needs about 24% growth a year for two years from that base (my arithmetic: 2,000 / 1,308, square root). The 23% H1 pace is just enough, with 7 to 8 points of that from price. Of the incremental growth, 60 to 70% is meant to come from product mix and 20 to 25% from innovation, automation and partnerships (p.10).
- Exports 15 to 20% of revenue, H1 near the top. Middle East is 80 to 85% of exports (p.17). So Middle East is roughly 12 to 17% of revenue (my arithmetic). That is a geopolitical exposure. The Mahindra Logistics call in this batch calls container spot rates "prohibitive" because of geopolitics.
- Fontech (the merged company, maintenance and repair) revenue is around ₹220 to 230 Cr per an analyst, no growth since the merger. Management says 8 to 12% is possible, not 20% (p.15).
- Acquisition in automation or robotics: "exploring", nothing near (p.8, p.14).
- ESOP covers about 50 people (p.15).
- The shipbuilding order book is "confidential" (p.16). Defence and nuclear were named in the question; the answer was not specific.

**Two readings.** Bull: 23% growth, mid-teens volume, a stated 12 to 13% margin target, no debt plan, steel already priced into the market. Bear: a third of growth is price, Q2 margin gave back what Q1 earned, short-term debt is up 100-fold, and the ₹2,000 Cr aim needs 24% for two more years. The separating observation: Q3 gross margin at or above 36% and short-term debt below about ₹10 Cr at March. Failing both confirms the bear case. Date: Q3 results, around late January 2027.

Price ₹1,371 (screener.in, 28-Oct-2026 06:10 IST). 52-week range ₹848 to ₹1,766, so the stock is 22% below its high. Market cap ₹2,385 Cr.

Cross-link: Transrail (on file, per the operator's review of 1-Oct) is a tower fabricator and a steel user. Ador's steel point (up 7 to 8% in Q2) and its demand read for power and infrastructure are relevant to Transrail's margin and tender flow. Other fabricators in this run's price list (Venus Pipes, Ratnamani Metals, Goodluck India) are steel-cost names too; I have not checked their master status.

### Fairchem Organics: profit headline is up 50%, but Q2 sales and volumes fell from Q1

Fairchem Organics is not in COMPANIES MASTER, so there is no thesis to test. Item 35 is the transcript of the 23-Oct-2026 earnings call (14 pages), filed on 27-Oct. The results themselves are not in this run's files. Every number below is [MGMT] spoken on the call. I read all of it.

Price context: ₹690 (screener.in, 28-Oct-2026 06:11 IST). 52-week range ₹428 to ₹847. The prices.json market cap is ₹869 Cr. The transcript gives no share count, so I cannot tie that figure to anything filed and I do not use it for a valuation multiple.

**What management said** (transcript p.2-3)
- Q2 FY27 revenue ₹167 Cr, up 49.7% YoY. EBITDA margin 10.5%. Net profit ₹10.2 Cr.
- H1 revenue ₹343 Cr, EBITDA ₹35.4 Cr (10.3%), net profit ₹21.2 Cr.
- Q2: 12,200 tons of raw material processed, 12,300 tons sold. H1: 24,600 processed, 25,800 sold.

**Testing the quarter** (my arithmetic from the stated figures, not filed)

Q1 is H1 less Q2:
- Revenue: ₹176 Cr. So Q2 revenue fell about 5% QoQ.
- Tons sold: 13,500. So Q2 volume fell about 9% QoQ.
- Tons processed: 12,400. So Q2 processing fell 1.6% QoQ.
- EBITDA: ₹17.9 Cr in Q1 (10.2%) against ₹17.5 Cr in Q2 (10.5%). Flat.
- Net profit: ₹11.0 Cr in Q1 against ₹10.2 Cr in Q2. Down 7%.

So the +49.7% is a year-on-year number on a weak base. The stated growth rates imply Q2 FY26 revenue of about ₹111.5 Cr and H1 FY26 about ₹242.6 Cr. That makes Q1 FY26 about ₹131 Cr, so last year's Q2 was itself a 15% dip on its Q1.

Price versus volume in the YoY number: realisation was ₹135 per kg in Q2, ₹130 in Q1 and ₹106 in Q2 FY26 (transcript p.5). That is +27% on price YoY. The remainder is about +17% on volume (₹111.5 Cr at ₹106/kg is about 10,500 tons against 12,300 tons). Price did more of the work than volume.

**Where this meets what the MD promised**
- The MD said the company is "on line" for 5% production growth every quarter (p.5, p.11-12). Processing fell 1.6% QoQ and sales volume fell 9%. The numbers do not show it.
- Capacity utilisation is "60% to 65%" (MD, p.4) and "close to around 65%" (CFO, p.11). The path to 85 to 90% rests on adding 5% a quarter. At 5 points a quarter from 65%, that is four to five quarters if it started now. It has not started.
- EBITDA margin: asked about 14 to 15%, the MD said "I would love to see not even 14% to 15%, I would love to see 15% to 18%" (p.10). Pressed on 16 to 18% he said "it's a capacity utilization margin put in" (p.10). The current margin is 10.3%. Moving from 10.5% to 15% at ₹135 per kg means EBITDA per kg rising from about ₹14 to about ₹20, which is about ₹7 Cr more a quarter on 12,300 tons. This is a wish, not guidance, and he did not give a date. When asked for a timeline, the answer was "5% to 10% growth every quarter".

**The raw material and duty story**
- The crude soybean oil import duty was cut from 16.5% to 11% (p.2). Management expects better raw material availability and lower prices. Nothing is quantified. Asked whether the benefit is already in H1 numbers, the MD said "we do carry some inventory" and the benefit would show in H2 (p.3, p.7).
- The call also says the company "suffered badly because of the 27% duty" (p.3). The two duty numbers (27% then, 16.5% before this cut) are not reconciled on the call. The earlier cut is already in H1; the new 5.5-point cut is the incremental one. The analyst's "8% margin hit" figure comes from the questioner, not from the company.
- Chinese dimer acid dumping "has gone down", which the MD attributes to a lower Chinese export incentive, with a weaker rupee also helping (p.4). He did not rule it out returning but said he does not expect it.
- Risk the analyst raised: if oil prices fall, the duty could go back up. The CFO said inflation is high and government is unlikely to increase it (p.11). That is opinion.

**The ₹12 Cr land purchase from the promoter** (p.7)

An analyst asked about "land worth ₹12 crore". The CFO said it was already used in operations and the workers' colony, and the company "thought of buying it from our promoter". This is a related-party transaction.
- Size: ₹12 Cr is 57% of H1 net profit (₹21.2 Cr) (my arithmetic).
- Not answered: price basis, independent valuation, audit committee and shareholder approval, and why the purchase was done now. The company had used the land without owning it.
- This is the item to chase in the full filing and the results note. The transcript does not carry the related-party disclosure.

**Other points**
- Working capital cycle 90 to 100 days for domestic sales, longer for export (p.8). Trade receivables are about ₹100 Cr per an analyst's reading (not confirmed by the company).
- No expansion for one to two years. Capex only if the new product works. "No" to using cash for debt reduction (p.12). Debt amount: NOT FOUND in the call.
- New product: a novel fatty-acid process, small plant, start slipped from the end of Q2 to Q3 (October to December), and "we don't expect that it will start functioning right from word go without any hiccups" (p.5-6). The plant is being modified after R&D feedback. Size of market, price, and capex: not given. The MD said it is "a very, very huge market" if it succeeds. That is not a number.
- Isostearic acid, the earlier launch: approval cycle was over 2 to 2.5 years, then Europe weakened and US duties rose, so "marketing has not reached its full potential" (p.12). US exports are on hold because of freight (p.5). The cosmetics push in Europe will be hit "in the near term" (p.8).
- Value-added products (linoleic acid for oil-field drilling, distilled dimer) may add 5 to 10% realisation (p.9).
- Minor inconsistency: the CFO gives tons sold as 12,300 in his statement and "12,400 odd" in Q&A (p.2, p.8). He also describes the half-year revenue growth of 41.4% as "against the corresponding quarter".

**Two readings**
- Bull. Realisation is up 27% YoY, dumping has eased, one duty cut is in and a second adds to H2 margin, and the plant runs at only 60 to 65%, so volume is the easy lever. The transcript supports this.
- Bear. Q2 sales, volumes and profit all fell from Q1. The 5% a quarter growth claim is not in the numbers. The margin target of 15 to 18% has no date or bridge. The new product has slipped a quarter and is unproven. The land deal with the promoter is unexplained.

The one observation that separates them: Q3 sold volume. Above about 14,000 tons (above Q1's 13,500) with EBITDA margin above 12% would show the duty cut and utilisation working. Below 13,000 tons with margin at 10 to 11% says the Q2 slowdown is the trend. The Q3 results settle it.

**Cross-link:** No company on file shares the edible-oil raw material chain. Aarti Drugs (T05) is on file as a specialty chemicals name, but the link is weak and I have not drawn one. The duty and inflation theme also touches United Foodbrands (T11), which is on file, though only through food costs.

### Share India Securities: ₹125 Cr NCD, ₹100 Cr more debt, and a merger dated back to April

Not in COMPANIES MASTER. Four filings.

**NCD issue (26-Oct, e857, pp.1-3).** The Finance Committee approved up to 1,25,000 NCDs of ₹10,000 each, so ₹125 Cr, on private placement, listed on BSE WDM. [FILED]
- The ₹50 Cr greenshoe is inside the ₹125 Cr, not on top of it (p.1 and p.2 item 3). The Screener summary reads as "₹125 Cr plus ₹50 Cr". That is wrong. The base issue is ₹75 Cr (my arithmetic: 125 less 50).
- Coupon 10.30% a year, paid monthly. Principal bullet at maturity. Tenure up to 21 months 5 days (p.2).
- Security: pari-passu hypothecation over all current assets and receivables, minimum cover 1.15x, plus personal guarantees of such promoters, promoter group members and directors "as may be mutually agreed" (p.2 item 8). So the NCD investors are lending against the book and the people.
- Default adds 2% a year (p.3).
- Annual interest at full size is about ₹12.9 Cr (my arithmetic: 125 x 10.30%).
- It draws on the ₹200 Cr debt programme the board authorised on 24-Jul-2026 (p.1). If the full ₹125 Cr is taken, ₹75 Cr of that programme is left (my arithmetic).

**Board agenda update (27-Oct, 7b3074, p.1).** The 30-Oct board will also consider "strategic restructuring initiatives" and raising up to ₹100 Cr more through NCDs, CPs or other debt. [FILED]
- Together with the ₹125 Cr above, that is ₹225 Cr of announced debt in four days, against a ₹200 Cr programme. The filing does not say whether ₹100 Cr is a fresh limit or sits inside the old one. That needs an answer.
- "Strategic restructuring" is not defined. It could be the Silverleaf merger follow-through, a subsidiary reshuffle or something new. Silence is information: the company chose to add it to the agenda one working day before the meeting and gave no detail.
- Context from the scheme document (e857, p.24): the company allotted ₹99.9 Cr of NCDs in June 2025 [FILED]. So this is a repeat, growing borrower: ₹99.9 Cr (Jun-2025), ₹200 Cr programme (Jul-2026), ₹100 Cr more proposed.

**Silverleaf merger, rectification order (7fe1ef, 40 pages).** The NCLT Ahmedabad bench, by order of 12-Oct-2026, changed the Appointed Date of the Silverleaf Capital Services amalgamation from 1-Oct-2023 to 1-Apr-2026 (pp.3-10). The company received the certified copy on 27-Oct at about 1:06 pm. [FILED]
- Why it changed: the Regional Director objected on 19-Jun-2026 to the 1-Oct-2023 date. Both boards moved to 1-Apr-2026 on 24-Jun-2026. The revised scheme was filed on 20-Jul-2026. The original sanction of 20-Aug-2026 had carried the old date, so the company had to apply for rectification. The Regional Director filed "no objection" on 7-Oct-2026 (pp.6-9).
- Consequence: Silverleaf's results come into Share India from 1-Apr-2026, which is the start of FY27. That means H1 FY27 will be restated to include Silverleaf. A date almost three years in the past would have reached back into FY24 to FY26 accounts and tax. That is gone.
- The scheme is not yet effective. It becomes effective when the certified order is filed with the Registrar of Companies, Ahmedabad (p.1 and cl.1.1.7). No effective date was filed.
- Terms: 500 Share India shares of ₹2 for every 1 Silverleaf share (cl.9.1, p.33). Silverleaf had 8,804 shares at 4-Sep-2025 (p.23). New shares: about 44.02 lakh (my arithmetic: 8,804 x 500). Share India had 21,88,25,530 shares at 4-Sep-2025 (p.24). Dilution about 2.0% (my arithmetic). At ₹177 the new shares are worth about ₹78 Cr (my arithmetic, not a filed valuation). ESOS options convert at 500 for 1 as well (cl.10.3).
- Accounting: acquisition method under Ind AS 103, assets at fair value on the Appointed Date, deficit to goodwill, surplus to capital reserve (cl.12, pp.34-35). [FILED] That differs from common-control pooling. It means goodwill could appear on the balance sheet. The consideration and Silverleaf's net assets are in the valuation report, which is not in this filing, so the size of goodwill is NOT FOUND.
- What Silverleaf is: a private company in high-frequency trading and statistical arbitrage with ML-based low-latency systems (cl.1.7). [FILED] It has no revenue or profit figure in this filing. NOT FOUND.
- The scheme says there is no change in promoter group or management (cl.1.5). Approval by public shareholders is a condition, with votes for needing to exceed votes against (cl.15.1). The order is sanctioned, so this has been done. [INFERENCE]

**NSE Clearing penalty (27-Oct, 552114, pp.1-2).** ₹1,00,000 plus ₹18,000 GST for breach of the Market Wide Position Limit (MWPL). The invoice reached the NSE portal at about 6:21 pm on 26-Oct. The company says no impact. [FILED] The money is trivial. The point is the type of breach. MWPL relates to open interest in stocks in the F&O ban regime. A broker with a proprietary HFT arm breaching it points to heavy position-taking. Which clients or which account is not stated.

**Read-across.** Four filings, one theme: the company is leaning harder on borrowed money and a bigger, faster trading book. Bull reading: the HFT merger adds a technology stack and the debt funds client margin, which earns. Bear reading: 10.30% monthly-pay paper with promoter guarantees is expensive funding for a broker, and MWPL breaches show risk limits being tested. The observation that separates them is the Q2 result on 30-Oct (if that is the meeting's main item, see below): net interest cost against interest income, and any change in prop-trading share of profit. NOT FOUND: the 23-Oct intimation is not in this set, so I cannot confirm results are due on 30-Oct.

Price ₹177 (screener.in, 28-Oct-2026 06:13 IST). 52-week range ₹115 to ₹233. Market cap ₹3,866 Cr. No position, no view. Cross-links to companies on file: none found.

### Puretrop Fruits: pre-tax profit down 31%, and the cash has gone into stock, a buyback and borrowing

Not in COMPANIES MASTER (formerly Freshtrop Fruits; fruit processing and export, plant at Nashik). Two filings (index 90 and 91) with identical content: board outcome letter, limited-review report and the results pages (dee8b5 and 3a45b4, pages 3 to 6 are images that I read). Standalone, unaudited, reviewed by FP & Associates (UDIN 26133589AJFHIF4433). Amounts in ₹ lakh unless stated.

**Quarter (p.4).**
| ₹ lakh | Q2 FY27 | Q1 FY27 | Q2 FY26 |
|---|---|---|---|
| Revenue from operations | 2,640.84 | 2,802.51 | 2,689.43 |
| Other income | 97.09 | 190.25 | 1.62 |
| Profit before tax | 256.30 | 531.48 | 373.48 |
| Tax | 18.19 | 74.95 | 108.28 |
| Profit after tax (continuing) | 238.11 | 456.53 | 265.20 |

- Revenue is down 1.8% year on year and 5.8% quarter on quarter (my arithmetic).
- Profit before tax is down 31% year on year. Strip out other income and it is ₹159.2 lakh against ₹371.9 lakh, down 57% (my arithmetic). Other income was 38% of this quarter's pre-tax profit.
- Operating EBITDA (profit before tax + finance cost + depreciation - other income) is about ₹325 lakh, a margin of 11.8%. A year ago it was about ₹538 lakh, 20.0%. Q1 FY27 was about ₹491 lakh, 17.5% (my arithmetic). The operating margin fell by 8 points year on year and 6 points quarter on quarter.
- The squeeze is "other expenses" at ₹810 lakh against ₹438 lakh, up 85% (p.4). Materials were better: materials plus inventory change was about 45.7% of revenue against 51.2% (my arithmetic). So gross margin improved and a large new cost line swallowed it. The filing does not say what that cost is. Freight and export logistics are the usual candidates [INFERENCE].
- PAT fell only 10% because tax was 7% of profit before tax this quarter, against 29% a year ago and 14% in Q1 (my arithmetic). A lower tax rate with large investment gains points to low-rate capital gains on funds [INFERENCE]. The filing does not explain. EPS ₹3.47 against ₹3.33 continuing. EPS is flat only because the share count fell, see below.
- Note numbering skips from 2 to 4. Note 3 is missing (p.4). Small, but unexplained.

**Half-year (p.4).** Revenue ₹5,443 lakh (flat, 5,484). Profit before tax ₹787.79 lakh against ₹432.12 lakh. H1 EPS ₹10.11 against ₹4.09 continuing. H1 FY26 also carried discontinued-operations profit of ₹1,560 lakh after tax, which is where FY26's headline came from: ₹1,803 lakh of the ₹2,529 lakh FY26 PAT, 71%, was from a discontinued business. The filing does not name it. NOT FOUND.

**Buyback, large and unannounced here (pp.3, 5).**
- Paid-up capital fell from ₹796.99 lakh to ₹686.99 lakh. At ₹10 face value that is 11,00,000 shares cancelled, 13.8% of the shares (my arithmetic).
- Cash flow shows buy-back ₹110 lakh, a further ₹2,130 lakh equity reduction, and ₹17.75 lakh of expenses. Total about ₹2,258 lakh, or ₹22.6 Cr (my arithmetic). Dividing by 11 lakh shares gives about ₹204 a share before costs [INFERENCE: the price is not filed]. That is above today's ₹181 and above the 52-week high of ₹200.
- That is 18% of today's market cap of ₹124 Cr (my arithmetic). Nothing in these two filings gives the buyback price, date, or promoter participation. If promoters did not tender, their percentage rose.
- The Q1 EPS of ₹6.65 uses the post-buyback share count (456.53 / 68.7 lakh). If the buyback closed late, a weighted count would give a different number. Not tested.

**Balance sheet and cash flow (pp.3, 5).**
- Inventory ₹5,291 lakh against ₹2,023 lakh in March, up ₹3,268 lakh. Receivables fell from ₹630 to ₹235 lakh.
- Short-term borrowing ₹1,199.55 lakh against ₹22.70 lakh. Long-term debt ₹86.74 lakh repaid. Finance cost ₹32.9 lakh in Q2 against ₹18.4 lakh in Q1.
- Operating cash flow minus ₹991.5 lakh for H1. Funding came from selling investments (₹3,255 lakh sold, ₹904 lakh bought), borrowing, and bank balances falling from ₹574 to ₹22 lakh.
- Liquid money (cash, bank, funds) minus borrowings fell from about ₹6,577 lakh in March to about ₹2,769 lakh, down ₹38 Cr (my arithmetic).
- Equity ₹122.7 Cr (₹686.99 + ₹11,585.27 lakh). Market cap ₹124 Cr, so the stock is at about 1.0 times book (my arithmetic).

**Two readings.** Bull: a seasonal inventory build for the export season, funded by short-term debt that unwinds; receivables are collected; the buyback shrank the share count by 14% and signals management thinks the stock is cheap. Bear: operating margin has collapsed from 20% to 12%, profit is propped by other income and a 7% tax rate, a third of the company's liquid reserve went in one buyback, and it is now borrowing against stock. The separating observation: Q3 operating EBITDA margin and inventory. Inventory back below about ₹3,500 lakh and operating margin back above 15% by the March quarter points to bull. Date: Q3 results, around end of January 2027. The seasonality in FY26 (Q2 at 20%, H1 at 10.7% on my arithmetic) means a single quarter proves little.

Price ₹181 (screener.in, 28-Oct-2026 06:12 IST). 52-week range ₹142 to ₹200. Market cap ₹124 Cr. Cross-links on file: none.

### Neochem Bio: CFO and CCO both replaced on results day

Not in COMPANIES MASTER. Two items, both without PDFs (downloads failed, HTTP 403), so this is from the summaries only.

- Item 77 (26-Oct): call notice. H1 earnings call on 29-Oct-2026 at 2:00 p.m., after the 27-Oct board meeting.
- Item 78 (27-Oct): the board approved the half-year results and appointed a new CFO and a new "CCO" effective 27-Oct-2026.

What is odd and worth reading in the full filing:
- Both changes land on the same day as the results. A CFO change that coincides with results can be benign, but it is where one looks for the reason the old CFO left, and whether it was before or after the limited review sign-off. The summary does not say who left or why.
- "CCO" is ambiguous in the summary. It could be Chief Compliance Officer or Chief Commercial Officer. The two would mean very different things; a change in the compliance chair together with the CFO is the sharper signal.
- The summary labels the results "H1 FY2026", while the call notice says H1 FY27. The period ended 30-Sep-2026 should be H1 FY27, so this is probably a summary slip, but the filing should be checked.
- The results numbers themselves are not available to me. They sit in the board outcome PDF, one day before the call.

### Pranik Logistics: CARE BB+ reaffirmed, limits raised 69%, collection period now 96 days

Not in COMPANIES MASTER. CARE Ratings press release dated 27-Oct-2026 (fc850f, 6 pages). It is a rating action on bank facilities. It is not a company filing of results. [FILED]

**Action (p.1).**
- Long-term fund-based cash credit: ₹48.00 Cr (up from ₹29.35 Cr), CARE BB+ Stable, reaffirmed.
- Long and short-term bank guarantee: ₹2.80 Cr (up from ₹0.65 Cr), CARE BB+ Stable / CARE A4, reaffirmed.
- Total rated limits ₹50.80 Cr against ₹30.00 Cr, up 69% (my arithmetic). BB+ is below investment grade. The previous reviews were 6-Mar-2026 and 24-Apr-2025, so this is a seven-month review, prompted by the larger limits.
- Total limits are 1.18 times the market cap of ₹42.9 Cr (my arithmetic).

**Numbers (pp.2-3).**
- FY26 revenue ₹159.73 Cr, up 52.5% from ₹104.76 Cr. Four-year CAGR about 38%. Q1 FY27 revenue ₹50.17 Cr (p.2) or ₹50.31 Cr (p.3 table): CARE gives two figures for the same quarter. Up about 28% year on year from ₹39.16 Cr.
- PBILDT (before interest, lease rentals, depreciation and tax) ₹15.26 Cr, margin 9.55% against 11.45%. Cause: a larger share of B2B business with lower margin.
- PAT ₹7.04 Cr against ₹6.44 Cr. Revenue grew 52% and profit 9%. PAT margin about 4.4%. Q1 FY27 PAT ₹2.04 Cr, PBILDT ₹4.64 Cr (9.2%).
- Gearing 0.99x against 0.51x. Debt to equity 0.24x. The gap shows most of the debt is working capital, not term debt. Interest cover 5.04x against 6.46x, and 4.55x in Q1 FY27. Total debt to gross cash accruals 3.62x against 2.03x.
- Collection period 96 days against 86. Working capital cycle 93 days against 82. Working capital utilisation 92% over the 12 months to August 2026 (p.3).
- Top three customers 62% of income (65% in FY25, 77% in FY24). Named: Qwik Supply Chain, ACC, Ambuja Cements. New: Flipkart group, BigBasket, Meesho, Piramal Pharma.

**What the rating agency itself flags.**
- A negative sensitivity is "elongation of the operating cycle to over 90 days" (p.1). FY26 is already 93 days. CARE reaffirmed anyway and says the cycle is "expected to remain elevated". So the stated downgrade trigger is already met on the last full year and was tolerated. The other triggers are revenue below ₹60 Cr (not close, Q1 alone is ₹50 Cr) and margin below 5% (not close).
- CARE expects debt to rise further: new vehicles and an ECLGS loan "in the current fiscal" (p.1). ECLGS is the government-backed emergency credit line scheme, usually taken by borrowers needing relief. That is notable for a company the agency calls "comfortable".
- Liquidity "adequate" at ₹16.55 Cr, but ₹15.23 Cr of it is lien-marked fixed deposits (p.3). Free liquidity is about ₹1.3 Cr (my arithmetic). Gross cash accruals ₹11.92 Cr against about ₹6 Cr of repayments due in FY27.
- Margin is before lease rentals, and the company has 46 leased warehouses (p.3). The margin after rent is lower. The comparison in this batch: Mahindra Logistics reports 5.9% EBITDA and 2.9% after removing the lease effect. How much lower for Pranik is NOT FOUND.
- The "comfortable financial risk profile" wording (p.1) sits oddly beside "moderated" capital structure and 92% utilisation. An internal tension in the release.

**Read.** Bull: growth is real, big-name customers are being added, rating held, limits enlarged by banks who know the account. Bear: profit is not following revenue, the business funds customers for 96 days, utilisation is at the ceiling, and the extra limits are the evidence of that. The separating observation: Q2 or H1 FY27 PAT margin against 4.4% and the collection period against 96 days. Limits being drawn to over 95% again after enlargement would confirm the bear case. Date: H1 results, due by mid-November 2026 under the usual deadline [INFERENCE].

Price ₹39 (screener.in, 28-Oct-2026 06:12 IST). 52-week range ₹36.2 to ₹85.8, so the stock is 55% off its high and near the low. Market cap ₹42.9 Cr. Cross-link: Mahindra Logistics (this batch) for the lease-rental gap; no company on file.

### Mitsu Chem Plast: secretarial auditor quits a year into five-year term

Mitsu Chem Plast is not in COMPANIES MASTER (notion_page null), so there is no thesis to test. It is a small BSE-listed company (scrip 540078, market cap ₹285 Cr). Three filings, all read in full.

- Resignation (idx 74, Reg 30, 26-Oct, 4 pages). Haresh Sanghvi, a practising company secretary (FCS 2259, CoP 3675), resigned as Secretarial Auditor with immediate effect on 26-Oct-2026 (p.1, p.3, p.4). The letter says he was appointed for FY2025-26 to FY2029-30, so he leaves after one audit cycle of a five-year term. [FILED]
- The stated reason in both the company note and his own letter is "pre-occupation with professional assignments and unavoidable professional commitments". The company says there is no adverse remark with a bearing on the financial or operational affairs. His letter says the reason given is the only one and that management gave full cooperation. The two documents agree.
- The company did not name a successor. It says the Board will appoint one "in due course". A listed company must have a shareholder-approved secretarial auditor (Reg 24A). Mid-term replacement may need a shareholder vote at the next meeting. I have not verified this, so check the rule before relying on it.
- Board meeting (idx 73). Results for the quarter and half year to 30-Sep-2026 on Saturday 31-Oct-2026 at the registered office. The trading window has been shut since 1-Oct (p.1).
- Call intimation (idx 75). Earnings call Tuesday 3-Nov-2026 at 11:00 IST, three days after results. The IR agency is Stellar IR. Management on the call: Manish Dedhia (MD and CFO) and Kashmira Dedhia (VP Finance and Accounts). The surname is shared. The filing does not state any relationship.

What is unusual. Nothing in the documents is alarming. The timing is the point: the resignation came five days before the results board meeting. A secretarial auditor does not sign the financial statements, so the resignation does not touch the accounts. Two small flags: the same person is both MD and CFO and signs every filing, and finance support looks thin on paper. Both are normal for a company this size and neither is new.

Who it matters to. Holders watching governance in small caps. Nothing here moves a valuation. The next real information is the 31-Oct results and 3-Nov call. The filings do not describe what the company makes, its Q1 numbers or its order position, so I have nothing to compare the coming quarter against.
