### HEG Advanced Materials: hydro subsidiaries hand the chair to the son and leave the CFO seat open

Not in COMPANIES MASTER, and the operator's own earlier note (1-Oct review) treats it as a peer reference only. This filing (c12e55, 4 pages, 26-Oct) is about two hydro-power subsidiaries: Malana Power Company (MPCL) and AD Hydro Power (ADHPL). Board meetings held 26-Oct. [FILED]

**What changes.**
- Riju Jhunjhunwala is appointed non-executive additional director and Chairman of both MPCL and ADHPL from 3-Nov-2026, "subject to approval of the Board of HEG Advanced Materials" (p.1, p.3).
- Ravi Jhunjhunwala, his father, gives up the Chairman post at both on 3-Nov. He is re-appointed Managing Director of MPCL for five years, 1-Nov-2026 to 31-Oct-2031 (p.1).
- Om Prakash Ajmera becomes Joint Managing Director and CEO of both for five years from 1-Nov-2026. He gives up the CFO post at both the same day (p.1-2, p.4).
- The relationship is disclosed: father and son (p.4).

**What it means.**
- This is a succession step. The father moves from Chairman to MD only, the son takes the chair. Riju is already Chairman, MD and CEO of HEGAM and of RSWM (p.3). One person now holds the top seat across a listed parent, a listed textile company and two hydro subsidiaries. [FILED for the posts]
- A CFO vacancy: Ajmera leaves the CFO seat at MPCL and ADHPL on 1-Nov. No successor is named. He is also Group CFO and a non-executive director of HEGAM (p.4). The filing gives no date for a new CFO. This is the open question. Between 1-Nov and an appointment, finance sign-off at both subsidiaries is unstated.
- Ravi Jhunjhunwala took an MBA in 1980-81 after a 1976 degree (p.3), so he is likely over 70 [INFERENCE]. A five-year Managing Director term at that age needs a special resolution of shareholders under company law (section 196(3)) [INFERENCE, not stated in the filing]. The filing says the appointments are board-level and does not mention shareholder approval. Ask.
- Riju's appointment is "subject to approval" of HEGAM's board, but that board approval is not in the filing. It is a formality inside a family-controlled group, but it is not yet done on paper.
- The profile text says Ravi "is currently Chairman, Managing Director & CEO of HEG Graphite Limited (now renamed HEG Limited)" while HEG Limited was "renamed HEG Advanced Materials" (p.3). So after the composite scheme the graphite business sits in a separately named company that took over the HEG Limited name, and HEG Advanced Materials is the vehicle with the Bhilwara Energy and hydro assets. This matters for anyone tracking graphite electrodes.
- Not in the filing: power capacity, tariff, revenue or profit of either subsidiary. NOT FOUND. Nothing here changes any operating number.

**Cross-link.** The Graphite India page on file mentions HEG only as a peer. The graphite electrode business now sits in the company that took the HEG Limited name, not in HEG Advanced Materials. If that peer reference points to the ticker HEGAM, it points to the wrong entity for graphite. Worth checking. The HEGAM subsidiary Replus's non-binding Indus Towers MoU (1-Oct review) belongs to the HEG Advanced Materials group, the same listed entity as this filing.

### Urban Enviro: results deferred, CFO pay raised, new JV for a Jalandhar tender

Not in COMPANIES MASTER. Two items (156 and 157, both 27-Oct), no PDFs (HTTP 403), so this is from the summaries only.

- Item 156, board outcome: the board approved a CFO pay hike, approved a joint venture for the Jalandhar waste tender, and deferred the half-year results.
- Item 157, "Agreements": a joint venture agreement.

Why this is the item to read first in this file:
- A board that defers results while raising the CFO's pay is a governance flag, though the two may be unrelated. Under SEBI LODR the results for the quarter ended 30-Sep must be out within 45 days, which is 14-Nov-2026 (my arithmetic). The deferral is not a breach yet. The thing to watch is whether the new date is before 14-Nov and what the reason is (auditor review not complete, a pending accounting issue, or admin delay). The summary gives no reason.
- The summary says "H1 FY26"; the period should be H1 FY27. Probably a summary label slip, but check.
- The JV: partner, shareholding split, project value, term and whether the partner is a related party are all unknown. A tender win for municipal solid waste usually means working capital and performance guarantees from a company with a market cap of ₹112 Cr, so the funding of the JV share matters.
- The CFO pay hike: amount and percentage are not in the summary. For a small company, the number and whether shareholder approval is needed are what count.

### Taurian MPS: 3.5 lakh shares allotted at ₹419, now ₹520

Not in COMPANIES MASTER. One item (144, 26-Oct), "Committee Meeting Updates", no PDF (HTTP 403). Per the summary, a committee allotted 3,51,200 shares on 26-Oct-2026 at ₹419, raising ₹14.72 Cr.

Check: 3,51,200 x ₹419 = ₹14.715 Cr, so the summary is consistent.

What is not known without the filing:
- Whether this is a warrant conversion from an earlier preferential issue, an ESOP exercise or a fresh preferential allotment. A committee-approved allotment at a fixed price points to a warrant conversion, but that is inference.

- Who received the shares. Promoter and non-promoter allottees mean different things for dilution.

Scale (my arithmetic, using market cap and price): ₹462 Cr / ₹520 gives about 88.8 lakh shares in issue, so the allotment is roughly 4% of the share count. That is a real dilution for a company this small.

### Eimco Elecon: new diaphragm-wall grab, no price, no order, no volume

Eimco Elecon (India) is not in COMPANIES MASTER (notion_page null), so there is no thesis to test. One filing (idx 27, 2 pages), read in full, plus its press-release page.

What it says [FILED]. On 27-Oct-2026 the company launched the DW-220 hydraulic diaphragm wall grab, category construction equipment, for the domestic market only (p.1). It is filed under the SEBI new-product disclosure format of 30-Jan-2026, which asks for just five fields: name, launch date, category, domestic or international, countries. The company gave exactly those five.

Specifications in the press release (p.2): excavation to 32 m, 120-ton grab closing force, bucket 0.8 to 1.3 cubic metres, bucket widths 500, 600, 800 and 1,000 mm, 320-bar hydraulic system, Isuzu diesel engine, ConSite telematics. It is aimed at metro and underground rail, deep basements, high-rise foundations, cut-and-cover tunnels, riverfront walls and dams.

What it does not say (silence is information):
- No price, no order or enquiry, no target volume, no revenue expectation, no development cost or capex.
- No local-content share. The release is framed as supporting "Make in India" and "Indian manufacturing capability", yet names an Isuzu engine. It does not say whether the engine, hydraulics or telematics are imported or licensed.
- ConSite is named without saying who supplies it. I recall the brand as a Hitachi Construction Machinery product but have not checked this, so treat it as unverified.

Who it matters to. The release's own description of the company is a "leading force in underground mining" with construction equipment as a secondary offering. A diaphragm wall grab is niche: it matters only as a foothold in foundation and metro works, a market led by established rigs. That is [INFERENCE]. For the existing mining story it is a side item with no number attached. Do not read it as a growth trigger until a first order or a revenue line appears.

Price context: ₹2,154 (screener.in, 28-Oct-2026 06:10 IST). 52-week range ₹1,406 to ₹2,475, so 13% below the high. Market cap ₹1,243 Cr.

Cross-links on file: Larsen & Toubro is on the price list and is a large builder of metro and underground works, so its project awards are the natural end-demand signal for this kind of equipment. Ashoka Buildcon and Pitti Engg are also on file. The link is by sector only. Nothing in the filing names a customer.

### S H Kelkar: cautionary letters, no penalty, for a one-working-day filing delay in February 2025

Not in COMPANIES MASTER. Filing 71adaf, 27-Oct, with BSE and NSE letters attached (pp.3-4). [FILED]

- BSE (1:32 pm) and NSE (4:32 pm) each sent a cautionary letter on 27-Oct-2026. The subject is the intimation of an investor or analyst meet on 3-Mar-2025, filed on 27-Feb-2025. The rule is at least two working days' notice, excluding the filing date and the meeting date.
- My arithmetic: 27-Feb-2025 was a Thursday and 3-Mar-2025 a Monday. The only working day in between is Friday 28-Feb. That is one clear working day, so the notice was one day short.
- Both letters say the lapse is "viewed seriously". NSE adds that any future aberration "will be viewed seriously and appropriate action would be initiated" (p.4). Neither levies a fine. The company states no impact.
- Unusual feature: the letters arrive about 20 months after the event. This looks like a back-dated review of old disclosures, not a response to anything new. Whether other companies got similar letters in the same sweep is outside this filing.
- What it does not say: who the investors were, why notice was late, whether other Reg 30 items from that period are under review. NOT FOUND.
- Governance footprint: low. It is a "first caution on record", and a repeat would draw action per NSE.

### Knowledge Marine: reclassification application filed, nine members dropped out, all negligible holders

Not in COMPANIES MASTER (ship repair, marine contracting). Filing 8dc201, 26-Oct, under Regulation 31A (2 pages). [FILED]

- Nine members withdrew and were removed from the application (p.1). Together they hold 180 shares: Pankaj Gurbani 150, Bharti Mukesh Daswani 30, and seven members with zero shares. So the dropouts are people holding nothing or almost nothing.

Seven holding zero shares withdrawing suggests they are being kept on the promoter list for family-tree reasons or because a condition was not met [INFERENCE]. Reclassification needs the exchanges' approval; the filing does not mention the shareholder approval step that the regulation also requires. Ask for the 19 and 21-Oct letters.
- Impact: the application is not approved. Until BSE and NSE rule, nothing changes in the shareholding pattern. A larger public float would be a positive for index and fund eligibility if the stakeholders reclassified are material; if they are small holders the effect is nil.

### Steamhouse India: exploratory MoU, no award, no money committed

Not in COMPANIES MASTER, so there is no thesis to test. This is an explanation of the filing.

**What was filed.** A press release dated 27-Oct-2026 (Steamhouse_India_..._d422d8.pdf, p.1-2). Steamhouse signed an MoU with the Haryana Enterprises Promotion Centre (HEPC), Government of Haryana. The MoU covers "industrial infrastructure opportunities involving a proposed investment of ₹500 crore" in Haryana. The example given is community boiler infrastructure for industrial clusters. Panipat and Sonipat are named as possible sites (p.2). [FILED]

**What it is not.** The release says in plain words that the MoU is exploratory, "does not constitute a project award or a commitment to implement a specific facility", and depends on feasibility, demand, environmental needs and statutory approvals (p.2). [FILED] The ₹500 crore is a "proposed investment", not a board-approved capex plan. No site, capacity, tariff, customer, funding mix or timeline is given. [INFERENCE] That silence matters: a community boiler plant needs a pipeline network and anchor steam customers before it is bankable, and none are named.

**Why it is of interest.** Steamhouse sells steam from community boilers over pipelines, buys and resells third-party steam, and runs a PSA nitrogen plant at Ankleshwar (p.3). The filing describes a Surat-based business and says the strategy is to reduce dependence on individual regions (p.3). Haryana would be a first step outside the home belt, but this is a state-facilitation MoU, not a customer contract. The release ties the idea to Delhi-NCR air quality and CPCB guidance on community boilers (p.2). That is context, not a mandate. [MGMT]

**Scale test.** ₹500 crore against a market cap of ₹3,887 Cr is about 13% (my arithmetic, not filed). The company's capex capacity and cash are not in this filing, so whether it can fund this is NOT FOUND here. The latest balance sheet and the IPO use-of-proceeds statement would answer it.

**Price.** ₹141 (screener.in, 28-Oct-2026 06:13 IST); 52-week range ₹91.5 to ₹144. The stock is within about 2% of its 52-week high on a non-binding MoU (my arithmetic, not filed). [INFERENCE] The market is giving credit for a headline.

**Cross-links.** No company on file shares this niche. The nearest theme is industrial energy infrastructure (Tata Power's BPCL 110 kV MoU is in today's index, item 143), but the business models differ.

**What would make it real.** A definitive agreement with a named site, capacity and anchor customers, plus a board capex approval.

Not in COMPANIES MASTER, so no thesis test. This is an explanation of the filing.

### Mishra Dhatu Nigam: MoU to recover nickel and cobalt from its own scrap

Not in COMPANIES MASTER, so no thesis test. This is an explanation of the filing.

**What was filed (27-Oct, pp.1-2).** MIDHANI (Ministry of Defence PSU), the Heavy Water Board (HWB) and the Bhabha Atomic Research Centre (BARC), the latter two under the Department of Atomic Energy, signed an MoU on 27-Oct-2026. The scope is production and recovery of cobalt and nickel from "plant reverts". Plant reverts are scrap and off-cuts from melting and forging. The MoU is a "framework for cooperation" to "explore and implement" recovery processes. It was signed by the CMD, the HWB chief and BARC's Associate Director, Materials Group. [FILED]

**What is unusual.** There is no money, volume, timeline, capex or revenue number. There is no statement of how much nickel or cobalt MIDHANI's reverts contain or what recovered metal is worth. The CMD calls it a "milestone" and says the aim is "viable, sustainable, and commercially competitive solutions" (p.2). That wording shows the commercial case is not yet proven. [MGMT]

**Who it matters to.** MIDHANI makes superalloys and special steels, where nickel and cobalt are main inputs. Recovering them from its own scrap could cut raw material cost and import exposure, but the filing gives no size to test that. HWB and BARC bring separation and process know-how. [INFERENCE]

**Materiality.** Low until a process, pilot plant and quantity exist. It is a technology-partnership headline, not an order or a result.

**Price.** ₹369 (screener.in, 28-Oct-2026 06:12 IST); 52-week range ₹267 to ₹482; market cap ₹6,907 Cr.

**Cross-links.** The release mentions battery use of nickel and cobalt. That touches the battery-materials theme on file through HEG Advanced Materials (a peer reference on the Graphite India page) and the telecom battery lane noted under Transrail. This MoU is about alloy-plant scrap, not batteries, so the link is thin. [INFERENCE]

### Premier Explosives: Rajasthan Global Securities crosses 5% by open-market buying

Not in COMPANIES MASTER, so no thesis test. The PDF has no text layer; both pages were read from images.

**What was filed (Reg. 29(1) SAST, signed 26-Oct, p.1-2).** The acquirer is Rajasthan Global Securities Pvt Ltd (RGSPL), Noida, signed by authorised signatory Lalit Dua. It is not a promoter or promoter group member and names no persons acting in concert. [FILED]
- Before: 25,33,269 shares, 4.71%.
- Bought: 1,64,297 shares, 0.31%, in the open market on 23-Oct-2026.
- After: 26,97,566 shares, 5.02%.
- Share capital: 5,37,61,195 equity shares of ₹2 each, unchanged. No pledge, warrants or other instruments.

**Arithmetic (my arithmetic, not filed).** 5% of 5,37,61,195 is 26,88,060 shares. RGSPL's 26,97,566 is 9,506 shares above that line. The purchase took it just over the 5% threshold, which triggers Regulation 29(1). The percentages check: 2,697,566 / 53,761,195 is 5.02%.

**Timing.** The trade was on Friday 23-Oct. The disclosure is dated Monday 26-Oct and reached the index on 27-Oct. That is inside the two working days the rules allow. No lateness.

**What it does NOT say.** Price paid, purpose, and plans for further buying are not disclosed. Part-B is blank by design and is not disseminated. RGSPL's ultimate owner is not stated. The name suggests a securities entity; that is inference only. [INFERENCE]

**Two readings.**
- Bull: an acquirer that held 25 lakh shares and added 1.64 lakh in one day is building a position on conviction.
- Bear: 0.31% in a day is small, and crossing 5% by about 9,500 shares looks like routine trading. Broker-type entities often cross and recross 5% as inventory moves.

The observation that separates them is the next disclosure. A further 2% change under 29(2), or the Dec-2026 shareholding pattern naming RGSPL as a public holder above 5%, would show accumulation. A fall back below 5% would show a trader.

**Price.** ₹588 (screener.in, 28-Oct-2026 06:12 IST); 52-week range ₹378 to ₹830; market cap ₹3,162 Cr. At ₹588 the 1,64,297 shares are about ₹9.7 Cr (my arithmetic at today's price, not the trade price). This is not a control-size stake.

**Cross-links.** None on file. MIDHANI (defence PSU) is in the same batch, with no tie beyond the defence sector.

### Sammaan Capital: ₹2.45 lakh NCD interest paid on time, tiny series
Sammaan Capital is not in COMPANIES MASTER. The Regulation 57 certificate (Sammaan_Capital_..._ab80f7.pdf, p.1) says monthly interest on two public-issue secured NCD series was paid in full on the due date, 27-Oct-2026. [FILED]
- INE148I07OU8: outstanding ₹25.5 lakh, interest ₹0.19390 lakh paid.
- INE148I07OT0: outstanding ₹279.556 lakh, interest ₹2.25721 lakh paid.
- Total interest ₹2.45111 lakh, which matches the screener summary. Record date 12-Oct, due date and actual date 27-Oct, last payment 27-Sep. No change in frequency, no delay, no redemption.
- Scale: the two series total about ₹3.05 Cr outstanding (my arithmetic). Market cap is ₹13,238 Cr. This is a legacy retail NCD tail, about 0.02% of market cap. It says nothing about the company's wholesale debt.
- Implied rate: ₹2.25721 lakh on ₹279.556 lakh is 0.807% a month, about 9.7% a year. The small series is 0.760% a month, about 9.1% (my arithmetic, simple annualisation). That is the cost of old retail paper, not today's borrowing cost.
- What it does not say: nothing on large institutional debt, ratings or asset quality. A clean routine payment is the baseline, not a positive. A missed one would have been the item.
- Price: ₹114 (screener.in, 28-Oct-2026 06:13 IST), range ₹111 to ₹193, market cap ₹13,238 Cr. It is 2.7% above its 52-week low and 41% below the high (my arithmetic). This filing does not explain that weakness.
- Cross-link: none on file.

## Cross-links

**Rules that bend at the line.** This window has five cases where a written rule and the evidence point different ways on the same day. Indiamart's exit trigger #6 fires as written, while every profit leg of the thesis got stronger. Route Mobile's 10% margin gate clears by ₹0.45 Cr only because of a ₹6.9 Cr add-back, and the same ₹448 price breaches the hard floor and meets the add rule. Syngene's T3 cash test fires on a half that was also negative last year. Unimech's cash gate misses 0.90x by 0.004x. The common fix is the same: each rule needs a stated basis (reported or adjusted, per quarter or cumulative, strict or seasonal) before the print, not after.

**Working capital is setting the cash gates.** Several H1 cash numbers today are working-capital stories, not operating ones. Pitti's H1 CFO was lifted by a ₹231.5 Cr payables stretch that management now calls permanent policy. Syngene's was sunk by a ₹382 Cr liabilities unwind. Route Mobile's fell to 15% of EBITDA on a ₹116.8 Cr vendor advance. Sky Gold's rests on a ₹345 Cr payables rise, and Wework India and L&T both got a large working-capital inflow (65% of L&T's Q2 operating cash flow). Any CFO/PAT gate on file should say whether it is tested as reported or excluding working-capital swings.

**Headline order books versus confirmed backlog.** Unimech's "₹855 Cr, up 8 times" is ₹266 Cr of confirmed POs plus ₹589 Cr of frameworks, and the confirmed part fell from June. That is the same intake-against-burn test as Transrail, whose board meets on 3-Nov and whose book leg already fails on the arithmetic. L&T's Infrastructure orders fell 13% on deferrals while its Power T&D arm was named among the big Q2 wins; L&T's H2 transmission commentary is the quickest read on Transrail's "tenders bunch into H2" bull case. Vivid Electromech's ₹307 Cr book also runs ahead of what its filed orders explain by about ₹45-50 Cr.

**Debt tested before or after equity money.** Raymond Realty, Embassy Develop, Venus Pipes and Sambhv Steel all carry the same open ruling: are debt tests counted before or after promoter or preferential money arrives? It got sharper today. Venus confirmed the ₹344 Cr repayment from its ₹372 Cr allotment lands in Q3. Embassy has received ₹284.68 Cr of warrant cash. Raymond now has three inputs (warrants, the ICICI AIF and the authority dues behind ₹17 Cr of quarterly interest). One standing ruling would settle all four.

**Mumbai power: Tata Power and Bajel.** Tata Power Mumbai gave Bajel the 400 kV GIS Dharavi/Ghatkopar order on 26-Oct. Tata Power's 27-Oct BPCL MoU and its planned 400 kV Trombay facility are the same kind of Mumbai EHV work. That is a possible future tender for Bajel, not an award. Neither filing moves either thesis today.

**Optical fibre price cycle.** HFCL says fibre prices rose about 20% in Q2 and preform spot rose about 600%. Sterlite reports the same quarter at the same margin, and its filed transcript hedges the 30% margin line from Q3. Corning's Q3 results around 30-Oct are the outside check. Paramount has a small telecom-cable line (3.2% of revenue) exposed to the same input.

**US-side export pressure.** SKM Egg cut Japan prices because of US egg supply, and Paramount's US exports fell 8.5% YoY. Both recoveries this quarter are domestic. If either export book was loss-making, a US restart could pull the blended margin down before it adds profit.

**Aerospace and defence supply chain.** Unimech posted a 39.8% EBITDA margin, which is the peer benchmark for Aequs's aerospace margin floor (monitorable 2), while Aequs fell 9.5% after results to sit 5.4% below its ₹231.55 promoter warrant price. Motilal crossed 5% in MTAR partly through defence index funds, so some of that bid follows the index, not the thesis. Sansera put ₹30 Cr of QIP money into an unnamed subsidiary, possibly its ADS (aerospace, defence, semiconductor) arm.

**Data-centre build-out.** Vivid Electromech's book is ₹307 Cr against ₹205 Cr at 31-Aug, with a repeat STT data-centre customer in the October orders, but its Ambernath plant slipped again to November. KRN's 30-Oct call should give the data-centre share against its 20% FY29 line. EPack lists industrial and data-centre capex overlap with KRN and Netweb as a concentration risk. Sterlite's MD cites hyperscaler capex. Demand looks intact; capacity and timing are the constraint in each name.

**Embassy Group: Embassy Develop and Wework India.** The Wework India shareholders' letter says the company is "promoted by the Embassy Group". Embassy Develop's promoter converted another 1.22 Cr warrants on 27-Oct. Nothing in these filings makes Embassy Develop a landlord or related party of Wework India, so treat it as a name to check, not a link.

**Steel and metals.** Gallantt Ispat's shutdown quarter (EBITDA ₹111 Cr against ₹185 Cr in Q1) and its September price rise are the TMT read-across for Sambhv Steel's 30-Oct call. Ador Welding says steel pass-through lagged, which cut its EBITDA growth to 9% on 23% revenue growth.

**Records that run on stale inputs.** Route Mobile's Entry/MoS properties still show the April values, so the holdings table compares price against ₹628 / ₹502, not the ₹579 / ₹463 set in August. Transrail's Position Size still says "add below ₹450", which ₹431 has already crossed. Blue Jet and Jay Bharat Maruti use share counts that disagree with the filed or market counts (by about 9% and about 2x). Northern Arc, Transrail, MPS, Zee, Adani Power and Embassy have tracker next-check dates set to mid-November for prints that land on 30-Oct to 3-Nov.

## Unknowns

**Resolved this session:**
- Indiamart: Q2 unique enquiries 27M, down 14% YoY. The Q1 -11% figure is the right one (FY26 arithmetic). The MonotaRO exit loss sat in Q1 other income (₹7.15 Cr consolidated, ₹20.0 Cr standalone). IndiaMart Finance Ltd started with ₹12.05 Cr of equity.
- Route Mobile: Heltar cost up to ₹33.66 Cr. India segment recovered (₹27.11 Cr profit against a ₹4.00 Cr loss). The weak H1 cash comes from a ₹116.8 Cr vendor advance. The ₹65 Cr IPO money stays on the Mumbai office object.
- Unimech: the Q4 FY26 finance-cost spike was one-off. Gross margin recovered. The ₹148 Cr figure is parent CCDs into a subsidiary; external Hobel cash out was ₹424.80 Cr. IPO money is fully spent.
- Raymond Realty: Q2 net debt ₹912 Cr final; net D/E 0.56x, gross 0.75x (the 0.13x on file is wrong). H1 CFO -₹147 Cr, inside the tracker line.
- Syngene: H1 CFO -₹143.2 Cr; net cash ₹1,392 Cr. First Bayview manufacturing agreement signed; drug-substance manufacturing in FY28.
- Permanent Magnet: the 49-50% QMPL stake line is in the company-filed transcript, not only the auto-transcript. No FY28 auto orders.
- Sterlite: net zero target is 2030, not 2050. The MD, not the CFO, answered all Q&A.
- SKM Egg: H1 powder volume 3,457 MT against 4,289 MT; ASP about ₹700/kg; the ₹300 Cr farm loan is sanctioned but not drawn.
- Pitti: management calls the payables stretch permanent policy and redefined the 17,000 MT castings guide as castings produced.
- Paramount: order book ₹731 Cr; exports fell 8.5% YoY; FII+DII 6.02%, so the UA multiplier is off.
- Embassy Develop: 71.3% of warrants converted, ₹284.68 Cr received.
- Blue Jet: bank debt fully repaid; CARE withdrew the rating.
- KRN: there is a Q2 call (30-Oct), so the 22-Oct "likely no call" read is superseded.

**Not resolved, and stated as the uncertainty of this read:**
- Indiamart: the unrounded active-buyer count (40M printed) decides #4 and the 8B trim watch. The Q2 call content is not in this batch.
- Route Mobile: who the ₹116.8 Cr vendor advance is with, and on what terms; why standalone receivables rose 62% in H1; whether the 28-Oct call gives any Proximus revenue KPI.
- Unimech: Hobel's Q2 revenue and margin; the basis of the June ₹280.3 Cr book; the date, customer and Reg 30 status of the USD 43M award letter.
- Raymond Realty: the balance of deferred dues to BMC, TMC and MHADA behind ₹36 Cr of H1 interest.
- Syngene: the Q2 research split (R2/T1) and whether FY27 guidance was cut again, both waiting on the 28-Oct call.
- Sical: whether the ₹81 Cr Tata Capital lease replaces or adds to the 30-Jul ₹72 Cr approval.
- Transrail: Q2 revenue and H1 CFO/PAT (3-Nov). The book-leg estimate assumes Q2 revenue near Q1's ₹1,702 Cr.
- KRN: Neemrana utilisation and H1 CFO/PAT (30-Oct call and 29-Oct board).
- Vivid Electromech: the deck itself was not read (403). The ₹307 Cr book's date, GST basis and split are unknown.
- Prices for Route Mobile, Unimech, Raymond Realty, Syngene and L&T are pre-results closes; the market reaction is not yet seen.
- All ten call audio links were not heard. Sona, Adani Power, Aequs and Netweb rulings wait on transcripts due about 2-Nov.

## Rulings needed

Live now, or due before a print in the next week:

1. Indiamart Inter., thesis-broken #6 (enquiries down more than 5% YoY for 2 quarters). Reading one: Q1 -11% was strike one, Q2 -14% is strike two, it fires and the action tier is EXIT. Reading two: #6 was carried as a RED monitor, not a live break, so it cannot drive an exit alone. Pending since 20-Oct.
2. Indiamart Inter., #4 and the 8B trim watch. Active buyers printed 40M, rounded. Does that count as under 40M or not?
3. Indiamart Inter., Monitor #11 RED leg (one associate write-down over ₹20 Cr). Standalone MonotaRO loss is exactly ₹20.0 Cr; consolidated is ₹7.15 Cr. Which basis?
4. Indiamart Inter., AR tier "TRIM to 1.5%". Met on Q1 and again on Q2 and not executed. Is that a deliberate override?
5. Route Mobile, Trigger 1 (EBITDA under 10% for 2 quarters, 50% exit). Adjusted basis: 10.04%, retired by ₹0.45 Cr. Reported basis: 9.75%, second quarter under 10%, it fires. Also rule whether the ₹6.9 Cr Masivian add-back counts (9.45% without it).

8. Route Mobile, properties. Update Entry/MoS from ₹628 / ₹502 to the 11-Aug body values ₹579 / ₹463?

## Three things you did not ask about

1. **Indiamart may have a second exit trigger already met.** Livekeeping carries a ₹5.26 Cr impairment allowance in the standalone books, unchanged since 31-Mar-2026, and IndiaMART put ₹6 Cr more into it in H1. Thesis-broken #11 says "Livekeeping impairment ANY amount". The allowance was on file before that rule was written. So the #6 ruling is not the only exit question on a held name; settle #11's scope at the same time.

2. **Raymond Realty's net debt leaves out about ₹750-850 Cr of debt-like dues.** Q2 finance cost includes ₹17 Cr paid to BMC, TMC and MHADA, about ₹68 Cr a year. At 8-9% that implies a deferred balance of about ₹750-850 Cr outside the ₹912 Cr net debt (analyst inference). Counted inside, the total passes the ₹1,500 Cr exit line. This decides whether a rule-based exit has already fired on a name priced ₹14 below the ₹611 entry ceiling.

3. **Route Mobile's group profit now rests mostly on other auditors.** In MSKA's first review quarter, subsidiaries reviewed by other auditors carried ₹65.43 Cr of Q2 PAT before consolidation adjustments, 79% of the ₹82.55 Cr group figure. In Q1 the same share was about 35%. On a held name where Trigger 1 hangs on ₹0.45 Cr and a ₹116.8 Cr vendor advance, the quality of the numbers matters as much as the line.

## Reading notes

Tool limits met:
- Notion was read only. notion-search was used 9 times to map unmapped names; notion-query-data-sources was not used. No quota error was met.
- No Trendlyne calls were made for prices. All 91 prices are from screener.in.
- No analyst listened to any of the 10 call audio or video links.
- 11 PDFs failed with HTTP 403 on both the main and fallback link. The cards for Vivid Electromech, Macpower CNC, Unihealth Hosp, Karnika Industries, Neochem Bio, Sakar Healthcare, Taurian MPS and Urban Enviro rest on the Screener type and summary only.

Facts from secondary sources rather than filings:
- Vivid Electromech: the ₹307 Cr order book, the November Ambernath date and the H1 revenue come from the Screener summary of a deck that could not be downloaded.
- Macpower CNC: the growth figures are the Screener summary, matched against the Q2 already on file. The ₹1,904 price is identical to the 26-Oct read and may be stale.
- Unihealth Hosp: the CSR press release is known only from its Screener summary.
- No PDF existed for the Asian Energy, Epigral and Sambhv Steel rating updates, the two Embassy Develop PIT items and the Gallantt promoter acquisition. Agency and action for the three ratings are unknown.
- Embassy Develop pledge: the 24.13% figure uses a secondary Sep-2026 shareholding read, not the filed encumbrance table.

Analyst reading limits, as stated:
- T08 read properties, Key Notes, the latest dated reviews and the gate sections for the long Embassy Develop and MTAR pages, not every older body section.
- T06 read HFCL properties, Key Notes and every section from 9-Oct onward; older middle sections were skimmed, since the item was a re-listed transcript.
- T07 fetched 2 of 15 Permanent Magnet tracker rows and took the other 13 from the 25-Oct page table; for Sterlite it fetched 5 of 6 rows.
- T13 did not fetch the Jay Bharat Maruti child page "FTTCP & Breakout Review" and used its Key Notes summary.
- N03 did not open page 9 of the Gallantt SAST image set (looks like a Part B continuation).
- L&T's Middle East shares (about 51% of Q2 inflow, 40% of the book) are chart readings from the text layer of deck pages 13-14 and should be checked by eye.

Corrections to earlier cards and records:
- N08 called Aarti Drugs "not on file". That is wrong: Aarti Drugs is in COMPANIES MASTER and T05 covers it.
- N05 and N06 could not confirm master status for some cross-link names. On file: Tata Power Co., Sical Logistics, Venus Pipes, Goodluck India, Transrail Light. Not on file: Wework India, Sky Gold & Diamonds, Ratnamani Metals.
- Permanent Magnet and Sterlite: the company-filed transcripts correct the 24-25 Oct reviews, which used auto-generated transcripts. Sterlite's net zero target is 2030, not 2050, and the MD, not the CFO, answered the Q&A. Sterlite's data-centre share is 33% of H1 per the CFO, so the 24-Oct chart reading of 53% looks like a label swap.
- KRN: the 22-Oct "likely no call" read is superseded by the 30-Oct call notice.
- Indiamart: the Q1 "-11% versus -16%" enquiry question is settled in favour of -11%.
- Raymond Realty: the 0.13x D/E on file is wrong; net is 0.56x and gross 0.75x.
- Unimech: the AR review logged Uniflux as a 100% subsidiary and the parent as debt-free; the H1 accounts show a small minority interest and a ₹12.02 Cr parent term loan.
- Screener summaries mislabel S Chand, Neochem Bio and Tanfac quarters as FY26 (should be FY27) and DIC India as Q2 (it is Q3 for a calendar-year company). The Share India NCD size and Dynamic Cables item 23 (out by 10x) are also wrong in the summary.
- Prices: the read time was shifted by an exact +9h30 after the PC clock moved to UTC-4. The true read was 28-Oct 06:09 to 06:14 IST.
